K K -theory of crossed products via homotopy theory
Ulrich Bunke

Abstract
In this paper we analyse for a G G - C ∗ C^{*} -algebra A A to which extent one can calculate the K K -theory of the reduced crossed product K ⁡ ( A ⋊ r G ) K(A\rtimes_{r}G) from the K K -theory spectrum K ⁡ ( A ) K(A) with the induced G G -action. We also consider some cases where one allows to use the K K -theories of crossed products for some proper subgroups of G G . Our central goal is to demonstrate the usefulness of a homotopy theoretic approach. We mainly concentrate on finite groups.



1
Introduction

Given a
C^{*}
-algebra
A
with a continuous action of a second countable locally compact group
G

one can form a new
C^{*}
-algebra called the reduced crossed product
A\rtimes_{r}G
. It is then a natural question whether one can calculate the

K
-theory spectrum
K(A\rtimes_{r}G)
in terms of
the
K
-theory spectrum
K(A)
with the induced action of
G
. This will of course be only possible in very simple situations, but in more general cases one may hope to calculate
K(A\rtimes_{r}G)
in terms of
the spectra
K(A\rtimes_{r}H)
for some proper subgroups
H
which would indicate an inductive approach.

Example 1.1
.

The prototypical example is the case of the group

G={\mathbb{Z}}
. There is a canonical assembly map

\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{B{\mathbb{Z}}}K(A)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}K(A\rtimes_{r}{\mathbb{Z}})

which turns out to be an equivalence.
This fact is a consequence of the validity of the Baum-Connes conjecture with coefficients for the amenable group
{\mathbb{Z}}
(see also
1.2
). But it can also be deduced from the more classical
Pimsner-Voiculescu long exact sequence
[
PV80
]
of
K
-theory groups

\dots\to K_{*}(A)\xrightarrow{{\mathtt{id}}-1_{*}}K_{*}(A)\to K_{*}(A\rtimes_{r}{\mathbb{Z}})\to K_{*-1}(A)\ ,

where
1_{*}
is the induced action of the generator
1
of
{\mathbb{Z}}
on
K_{*}(A)
.

∎

Example 1.2
.

For general locally compact groups
G
the Baum-Connes conjecture with coefficients (see e.g.
[
GAJV19
]
for a recent survey) predicts for any
G
-
C^{*}
-algebra
A
an equivalence

\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{G_{\mathcal{C}\mathrm{pt}}\mathbf{Orb}}K^{G}_{A}\xrightarrow{\simeq}K(A\rtimes_{r}G)\ .

(1.1)

Here
G_{\mathcal{C}\mathrm{pt}}\mathbf{Orb}
is the full subcategory of the orbit
\infty
-category of
G
on orbits
with compact stabilizers, and
K_{A}^{G}
is a spectrum valued functor on
G\mathbf{Orb}
associated to
A
. It sends the orbit
G/L
to the spectrum
K_{A}^{G}(G/L)\simeq\mathrm{KK}^{G}(C_{0}(G/L),A)
. For open (and closed, of course) subgroups
L
the latter can be identified with
K(A\rtimes_{r}L)
using the adjunctions (
2.9
) and (
2.16
).

In the case of a discrete group
G
the orbit category
G\mathbf{Orb}
is ordinary and the construction of this functor is due to
[
DL98
]
(with corrections by
[
Joa03
]
), see also

[
Kra20
]
or
[
BELa
, Sec. 16]
. It has been shown by
[
Kra20
]
(see also
[
BELa
, Sec. 16]
) that the Baum-Connes-Higson assembly map featuring the classical formulation of the Baum-Connes conjecture is equivalent to the Davis-Lück assembly map appearing in (
1.1
).

It is known that the Baum-Connes conjecture with coefficients holds for amenable groups, hyperbolic groups and many others, but it is false in general
[
HLS02
]
.

If the Baum-Connes conjecture with coefficients holds for
G
, then it reduces the problem of calculating
K(A\rtimes_{r}G)

to the calculation of the spectra
K(A\rtimes_{r}L)
for all compact subgroups
L
of
G
and the homotopy theoretic problem of the
calculation of a colimit of a spectrum-valued functor over
G_{\mathcal{C}\mathrm{pt}}\mathbf{Orb}
.

∎

As explained in
1.2
the Baum-Connes conjecture takes the
K
-theories of crossed products by compact groups as an input in order to calculate the
K
-theory of crossed products by non-compact groups.
In contrast, in the present paper we are interested in the calculation of the
K
-theory of crossed products by compact groups themselves using homotopy theoretic methods. We will mainly concentrate on finite groups, i.e., the case of discrete compact groups.

We will first consider cases where one can calculate the spectrum
K(A\rtimes_{r}G)
from the spectrum
K(A)
with its induced
G
-action. Later we study the more complicated situation
where one also must take crossed products by subgroups of
G
into account.

Example 1.3
.

Assume that
G
is discrete and that
the
G
-
C^{*}
-algebra
A
is induced from the trivial subgroup, i.e.,
A\cong C_{0}(G,B)
with the
G
-action by left translations for some
C^{*}
-algebra
B
. Then
we have an equivalence

\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{BG}K(A)\simeq K(A\rtimes_{r}G)\ .

(1.2)

In order to see this one can calculate both sides explicitly.
For the right-hand side we employ Green’s imprimitivity theorem:

K(A\rtimes_{r}G)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{bwefljrfopwerfrerfwerfwreferf}}}{{\simeq}}K(B)\ .

For the left-hand side we observe that

A\cong\bigoplus_{G}B
in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}})
with the
G
-action by left translations on the index set. Since the
K
-theory functor preserves sums we get

K(A)\simeq\bigoplus_{G}K(B)
in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,{\mathbf{Mod}}(KU))
and can conclude that

\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{BG}K(A)\simeq K(B)\ .

∎

If
G
is finite and
A
is a
G
-
C^{*}
-algebra, then the Rohklin property (in the form introduced by
[
Izu04
]
,
2.41
) says that
A
is induced from the trivial subgroup in an asymptotic sense.
The following proposition can thus be considered as an extension of
1.3
.

Proposition 1.4

(
2.44
)
.

If
G
is finite and
A
is a
G
-
C^{*}
-algebra with the Rokhlin property, then the assembly map is an equivalence

\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{BG}K(A)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}K(A\rtimes_{r}G)\ .

A variety of examples of
G
-
C^{*}
-algebras with the Rokhlin property is given in
[
Izu04
]
, see also

2.53
below.

Remark 1.5
.

As indicated in the examples above we consider a homotopy theoretic formula
for the spectrum
K(A\rtimes_{r}G)
as an eligible solution. Our result reduces the
calculation of the
K
-theory groups
K_{*}(A\rtimes_{r}G)
to homotopy theoretic calculations
which still might be a complicated task involving spectral sequences and similar, see e.g. the open problem mentioned in
1.25
. But there is are situations which will be discussed in detail in
Section

2.5
below where this calculation is straightforward.
See also
1.17
and
1.18
for further concrete cases.

∎

A natural framework for the
G
-equivariant homotopy theory of
C^{*}
-algebras is the stable
\infty
-category
\mathrm{KK}^{G}
representing equivariant Kasparov
KK
-theory. We will review the basic features of this category
in
Section

2.1
. In particular, it has a symmetric monoidal structure whose tensor unit will be denoted by
{\mathbf{1}}_{\mathrm{KK}^{G}}
. The commutative ring spectrum

R(G):={\mathtt{map}}_{\mathrm{KK}^{G}}({\mathbf{1}}_{\mathrm{KK}^{G}},{\mathbf{1}}_{\mathrm{KK}^{G}})

(1.3)

is the homotopy theoretic version of the representation ring of
G
. As an object in presentable stable
\infty
-categories
\mathbf{Pr}^{L}_{\mathrm{st}}
the category
\mathrm{KK}^{G}
becomes a commutative algebra in modules over the commutative algebra
{\mathbf{Mod}}(R(G))
in
\mathbf{Pr}^{L}_{\mathrm{st}}
, the presentably symmetric monoidal category of modules over the ring
R(G)
. This is a neat way to say that
\mathrm{KK}^{G}
has a highly coherent enrichment over
{\mathbf{Mod}}(R(G))
. In particular,
the mapping spectra in
\mathrm{KK}^{G}
become
R(G)
-modules.
The
{\mathbf{Mod}}(R(G))
-module structure of
\mathrm{KK}^{G}
provides
a bifunctor

-\otimes-:{\mathbf{Mod}}(R(G))\times\mathrm{KK}^{G}\to\mathrm{KK}^{G}

(1.4)

which preserves colimits in each argument and is characterized by the property that
R(G)\otimes-
is equivalent to identity. In particular, any element
\sigma
in
\pi_{0}R(G)
gives rise to an object-preserving endofunctor of
\mathrm{KK}^{G}
,
i.e., it acts as an endomorphism on any object in a way which is compatible with morphisms.

The equivariant
K
-theory functor for
C^{*}
-algebras factorizes over
\mathrm{KK}^{G}
:

K^{G}:GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{kk}^{G}}\mathrm{KK}^{G}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{K}^{G}}{\mathbf{Mod}}(R(G))\ ,

where
\mathrm{K}^{G}:={\mathtt{map}}_{\mathrm{KK}^{G}}({\mathbf{1}}_{\mathrm{KK}^{G}},-)
.
The
R(G)
-module structure on
K
-theory plays an important role in this note since we want to consider
localizations and completions at elements in
\pi_{0}R(G)
.
In the case of the trivial group we will omit the superscript
G

in the notations for
K
and
KK
-theory functors.

The homotopy theoretic point of view leads to an immediate generalization of
1.3
.
We shall see that the map inducing the equivalence (
1.2
) comes from a natural transformation (
2.32
) of colimit preserving functors from

\mathrm{KK}^{G}
to
{\mathbf{Mod}}(KU)
. The equivalence can therefore be extended from induced
C^{*}
-algebras to
G
-
C^{*}
-algebras
whose
\mathrm{KK}^{G}
-class belongs to the localizing subcategory
\langle{\mathtt{Ind}}^{G}(\mathrm{KK})\rangle
generated by
the image of the induction functor
{\mathtt{Ind}}^{G}:\mathrm{KK}\to\mathrm{KK}^{G}
.
Even more generally we could take the
\mathrm{KK}^{G}
-classes themselves as parameters.
Ideas of this kind in the context of the triangulated equivariant
KK
-category have first been used in
[
MN06
]
in connection with the Baum-Connes conjecture.

For
A
in
\mathrm{KK}^{G}
we use the notation
\widehat{\mathrm{Res}}^{G}(A)
for the object in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,\mathrm{KK})
representing the underlying
\mathrm{KK}
-class of
A
with the induced
G
-action, see (
2.7
) for the precise definition. Note that for a
G
-
C^{*}
-algebra we keep writing
K(A)
for the
K
-theory spectrum with the induced
G
-action.

Proposition 1.6

(
2.7
)
.

Assume that
G
is discrete. If
A
is in
\langle{\mathtt{Ind}}^{G}(\mathrm{KK})\rangle
, then we have an equivalence

\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{BG}\mathrm{K}(\widehat{\mathrm{Res}}^{G}(A))\simeq\mathrm{K}(A\rtimes_{r}G)\ .

(1.5)

Remark 1.7
.

For a
G
-
C^{*}
-algebra
A

1.6
implies the equivalence (
1.2
)
under the assumption that the object
\mathrm{kk}^{G}(A)
can be obtained as a colimit of a diagram of
objects in the image of the induction functor. The case considered in
1.3

corresponds to the simple situation where
\mathrm{kk}^{G}(A)
itself is in this image.

The statement of
1.6
in the form of an isomorphism

\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{W\subseteq EG}\mathrm{KK}^{G}_{*}(C_{0}(W),A)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}K_{*}(A\rtimes_{r}G)

(1.6)

of
K
-theory groups
for
G
-
C^{*}
-algebras
A
such that
\mathrm{kk}^{G}(A)\in\langle{\mathtt{Ind}}^{G}(A)\rangle
is a well-known fact. Here the
colimit runs over the finite
G
-CW-subcomplexes of a
G
-CW model for the classifying space
EG
of
G
.
But note that the identification of the left-hand sides of (
1.6
) and (
1.2
) is a non-trivial matter and similar to the identifications of the domains of the Baum-Connes and the Davis-Lück assembly maps in
[
Kra20
]
, or using Paschke duality in
[
BELa
]
. We refer to

2.8
for further references.

Even more generally, using the notation introduced in
Section

2.4
and the method from the proof of
1.4
, one can show that the equivalence (
1.2
)
holds for
A
in
\mathrm{KK}^{G}
under the assumption that
c^{G}(A)
(see (
2.22
) for
c^{G}
) belongs to closure of
\langle{\mathtt{Ind}}^{G}(\mathrm{E}_{\oplus})\rangle
under universal phantom retracts.

∎

The following proposition characterizes a class of examples where the assumption of
1.6
is satisfied.
If
n
is an integer, then it induces an endomorphism
n{\mathtt{id}}_{A}
of any object
A
of a pre-additive (so in particular of a stable)
\infty
-category. If this endomorphism is an equivalence, then we say that
n
acts as an equivalence on
A
. Let
\rho
in
\pi_{0}R(G)
be the class of the left-regular representation of
G
on
L^{2}(G)
in the representation ring. Then
\rho
acts as an endomorphism of
A
and we could consider the condition that this endomorphism is an equivalence.

Let
A
be an object of
\mathrm{KK}^{G}
.

Proposition 1.8

(
2.9
)
.
1
1

1

The author thanks S. Nishikawa for a discussion about examples helped to locate a mistake in the statement of this proposition in an earlier version of this paper.

We assume that
G
is finite.
The following assertions are equivalent.

1.

\rho
acts as an equivalence on
A
.

2.

We have
A\in\langle{\mathtt{Ind}}^{G}(\mathrm{KK})\rangle
and
|G|
acts an equivalence on
A
.

Remark 1.9
.

Under the equivalent conditions of

1.8
we have the equivalence

\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{BG}\mathrm{K}(\widehat{\mathrm{Res}}^{G}(A))\simeq\mathrm{K}(A\rtimes_{r}G)\ .

In addition we know
that
K(\widehat{\mathrm{Res}}^{G}(A))
is projective by
2.49
so that we also have an isomorphism of
K
-theory groups

\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{BG}\mathrm{K}_{*}(\widehat{\mathrm{Res}}^{G}(A))\simeq\mathrm{K}_{*}(A\rtimes_{r}G)\ .

∎

In general the spectrum
K(A\rtimes_{r}G)
is not determined by the spectrum
K(A)
with its induced
G
-action alone. We now start to
consider situations where for the calculation of
K(A\rtimes_{r}G)
we need to take the contributions of crossed products for some proper subgroups of
G
into account. To this end,
in
3.7
we construct a functor

\mathrm{K}^{G}_{-}:\mathrm{KK}^{G}\times G\mathbf{Orb}^{{\mathtt{comp}}}\to{\mathbf{Mod}}(R(G))\ ,\quad(A,S)\mapsto\mathrm{K}^{G}_{A}(S)\ .

It is an analogue of the functor
K^{G}_{A}
appearing in
1.2
. In particular, for a compact group
G
and a cofinite subgroup
L
by
3.15
it also has the values
\mathrm{K}^{G}_{A}(G/L)\simeq\mathrm{K}(A\rtimes_{r}L)
. This equivalence equips the
KU
-modules
\mathrm{K}(A\rtimes_{r}L)
with
R(G)
-module structures.

If
G
is finite, then by
3.9
the
KU
-module
\mathrm{K}^{G}_{A}(G)
with the
G
-action induced by right-multiplication on
G
is equivalent to
\mathrm{K}(\widehat{\mathrm{Res}}^{G}(A))
, i.e., the
KU
-module
\mathrm{K}(A)
with
G
-action induced by functoriality. The functor
\mathrm{K}^{G}_{A}
thus interpolates between the (naive)
G
-spectrum
\mathrm{K}(\widehat{\mathrm{Res}}^{G}(A))\simeq\mathrm{K}^{G}_{A}(G)
and the spectrum
\mathrm{K}(A\rtimes_{r}G)\simeq\mathrm{K}^{G}_{A}(*)
.

We now restrict to the case of finite groups
G
. Let
G_{\mathcal{P}\mathrm{rp}}\mathbf{Orb}
denote the full subcategory of the orbit category of
G
on the orbits whose stabilizer is a proper subgroup of
G
.
We then analyse the cofibre sequence

\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{G_{\mathcal{P}\mathrm{rp}}\mathbf{Orb}}\mathrm{K}^{G}_{-}\to\mathrm{K}^{G}_{-}(*)\to\mathrm{Cof}^{G}(-)

(1.7)

of functors from
\mathrm{KK}^{G}
to
{\mathbf{Mod}}(R(G))
, where

\mathrm{Cof}^{G}
is defined as the cofibre. The evaluation of
the first map at
A
in
\mathrm{KK}^{G}
is called the assembly map for the functor
\mathrm{K}^{G}_{A}
and the family
\mathcal{P}\mathrm{rp}
of proper subgroups. Since
\mathrm{K}^{G}_{A}(*)\simeq\mathrm{K}(A\rtimes_{r}G)
this sequence suggests
an inductive approach to the calculation of
\mathrm{K}(A\rtimes_{r}G)
in terms of the cofibre and
a colimit of
K
-theories of crossed products
\mathrm{K}^{G}_{A}(G/L)\simeq\mathrm{K}(A\rtimes_{r}L)
by the proper subgroups
L
of
G
.
We are in particular interested in situations where this cofibre is trivial.

Here is a very simple instance where this is the case. We let
\langle{\mathtt{Ind}}_{\mathcal{P}\mathrm{rp}}^{G}\rangle
denote the localizing subcategory of
\mathrm{KK}^{G}
generated by the collection of
{\mathtt{Ind}}_{L}^{G}(\mathrm{KK}^{L})
for all proper subgroups
L
of
G
.

Proposition 1.10

(
3.17
)
.

If
G
is finite and
A
is in
\langle{\mathtt{Ind}}_{\mathcal{P}\mathrm{rp}}^{G}\rangle
, then the assembly map is an equivalence

\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{G_{\mathcal{P}\mathrm{rp}}\mathbf{Orb}}\mathrm{K}^{G}_{A}\xrightarrow{\simeq}\mathrm{K}(A\rtimes_{r}G)\ .

(1.8)

Remark 1.11
.

If
A={\mathtt{Ind}}_{L}^{G}(B)
for some
B
in
\mathrm{KK}^{L}
and proper subgroup
L
of
G
, then
the right-hand side of (
1.8
) can of course be calculated by Green’s imprimitivity theorem:

\mathrm{K}(B\rtimes_{r}L)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{bwefljrfopwerfrerfwerfwreferf}}}{{\simeq}}\mathrm{K}({\mathtt{Ind}}_{L}^{G}(B)\rtimes_{r}G)

and the spectrum on the left is one of the values of the functor contributing to the colimit in (
1.8
). So the
1.10
is not very useful for the calculation of the right-hand side of (
1.8
) in terms of the colimit, but it might be used in the reverse direction to extract some information about the structure of the colimit on the left-hand side.

∎

We continue to assume that
G
is finite.
We let

V
be the orthogonal complement of the trivial representation in the finite-dimensional
G
-representation
L^{2}(G)
.
We then define the element
\xi:=\Lambda_{-1}(V)
in
\pi_{0}R(G)
.
In
4.1
we further define the endofunctors
(-)[\xi^{-1}]
and
S_{\xi}:={\mathrm{Fib}}({\mathtt{id}}\to(-)[\xi^{-1}])
of any module category over

{\mathbf{Mod}}(R(G))
.

Theorem 1.12

(
Theorem

4.11
)
.

We assume that
G
is finite.
The cofibre sequence (
1.7
) is equivalent to the cofibre sequence

S_{\xi}(\mathrm{K}^{G}_{-}(*))\to\mathrm{K}^{G}_{-}(*)\to\mathrm{K}^{G}_{-}(*)[\xi^{-1}]

(1.9)

of functors from
\mathrm{KK}^{G}
to
{\mathbf{Mod}}(R(G))
.

The main insight given by
Theorem

1.12
is that
\xi
acts an equivalence on the values of the cofibre in (
1.7
). If
\xi=0
, then we could conclude that
\mathrm{Cof}^{G}\simeq 0
.
In
4.12
we will observe that if
G
is finite and not cyclic, then
\xi=0
.

Corollary 1.13
.

If
G
is finite and not cyclic, then the assembly map is an equivalence.

\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{G_{\mathcal{P}\mathrm{rp}}\mathbf{Orb}}\mathrm{K}^{G}_{A}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}\mathrm{K}(A\rtimes_{r}G)

for every
A
in
\mathrm{KK}^{G}
.

Let
G_{\mathcal{C}\mathrm{yc}}\mathbf{Orb}
denote the full subcategory of the orbit category of
G
on orbits whose stabilizer is a cyclic subgroup. By an inductive procedure using
1.13
in the induction step we can then deduce:

Proposition 1.14

(
4.13
)
.

If
G
is finite, then
we have an equivalence

\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{G_{\mathcal{C}\mathrm{yc}}\mathbf{Orb}}\mathrm{K}^{G}_{A}\xrightarrow{\simeq}\mathrm{K}(A\rtimes_{r}G)

for every
A
in
\mathrm{KK}^{G}
.

This result reduces the calculation of
\mathrm{K}(A\rtimes_{r}G)
to the calculation of the
K
-theory of crossed products by cyclic groups
and the colimit of a spectrum-valued functor over
G_{\mathcal{C}\mathrm{yc}}\mathbf{Orb}
.
For
A={\mathbf{1}}_{\mathrm{KK}^{G}}
this result is similar in spirit to the classical Artin- and Brower induction theorems.

Remark 1.15
.

1.14
can also be deduced from the results of
[
MNN19
]
. To this end one observes that

\mathrm{K}^{G}_{A}
(considered as a
\mathbf{Sp}
-valued functor) refines to spectral Mackey functor
2
2

2

The extension of
\mathrm{K}^{G}_{A}
to a Mackey functor will be discussed in a subsequent paper.
. This refinement has furthermore a lax symmetric monoidal refinement in the variable
A
and therefore becomes a module over
\mathrm{K}^{G}_{{\mathbf{1}}_{\mathrm{KK}^{G}}}
. The latter spectral Mackey functor can
3
3

3

In order to make this precise we would need a precise definition of what the usual usual genuine equivariant topological
K
-theory spectrum
KU^{G}
as a commutative algebra in spectral Mackey functors is. The reference
[
MNN19
]
is vague at this point.
be identified with the usual genuine equivariant topological
K
-theory spectrum
KU^{G}
. The assertion of
1.14
for
\mathrm{K}^{G}_{{\mathbf{1}}_{\mathrm{KK}^{G}}}
is then the same as
[
MNN19
, Prop. 5.7]
. It is easy to see that it implies
1.14
also for all modules over
\mathrm{K}^{G}_{{\mathbf{1}}_{\mathrm{KK}^{G}}}
.

∎

In the following we consider situations where the cofibre in the sequence (
1.7
), or equivalenty the third term in (
1.9
), may be non-trivial but splits off as a summand. Let
p
be a prime,
k
be in
{\rm Nature}\setminus\{0\}
, and let
A
be in
\mathrm{KK}^{C_{p^{k}}}
.

Proposition 1.16

(
5.6
)
.

If
p
acts as an equivalence in
A
, then the sequence

S_{\xi}(\mathrm{K}^{C_{p^{k}}}_{A}(*))\to\mathrm{K}^{C_{p^{k}}}_{A}(*)\to\mathrm{K}^{C_{p^{k}}}_{A}(*)[\xi^{-1}]

splits naturally.

Under the assumptions of
1.16

we thus have an equivalence

S_{\xi}(\mathrm{K}^{C_{p^{k}}}_{A}(*))\oplus K(A\rtimes_{r}C_{p^{k}})[\xi^{-1}]\simeq K(A\rtimes_{r}C_{p^{k}})

(1.10)

If
k=1
, then

S_{\xi}(\mathrm{K}^{C_{p}}_{A}(*))\simeq\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{BC_{p}}\mathrm{K}(\widehat{\mathrm{Res}}^{C_{p}}(A)))\ ,

and the
homotopy groups of this colimit can be calculated using

2.49
:

\pi_{*}(S_{\xi}(\mathrm{K}^{C_{p}}_{A}(*)))\cong\pi_{*}(\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{BC_{p}}\mathrm{K}(\widehat{\mathrm{Res}}^{C_{p}}(A)))\cong\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{BC_{p}}\mathrm{K}_{*}(\widehat{\mathrm{Res}}^{C_{p}}(A))\ .

(1.11)

For
k>1
the calculation of this summand is more complicated and involves a colimit over
C_{p^{k},\mathcal{P}\mathrm{rp}}\mathbf{Orb}
.

In general the homotopy groups of the second summand
K(A\rtimes_{r}C_{p^{k}})[\xi^{-1}]
in (
1.10
)
are still complicated to calculate. In the following we provide an interesting class of examples
where this calculation is possible.

Example 1.17
.

This example is added in order to demonstrate that the calculation of the
K
-theory of crossed products using the decomposition (
1.10
) confirms known results. We consider the group
G:=C_{p}
.
The simplest object of
\mathrm{KK}^{C_{p}}
on which
p
acts as an equivalence is of course

A={\mathbf{1}}_{\mathrm{KK}^{C_{p}}}[p^{-1}]
. Then
\mathrm{K}(\widehat{\mathrm{Res}}^{C_{p}}(A))\simeq\mathrm{triv}^{C_{p}}(KU[p^{-1}])
and the
first summand in (
1.10
) is given by
\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{BC_{p}}\mathrm{triv}^{C_{p}}(KU[p^{-1}])\simeq KU[p^{-1}]
.
The second summand is given as a
KU
-module by

\mathrm{K}({\mathbf{1}}_{\mathrm{KK}^{C_{p}}}[p^{-1}]\rtimes_{r}C_{p})[\xi^{-1}]\simeq R(C_{p})[p^{-1}][\xi^{-1}]\simeq KU[p^{-1}]^{\oplus(p-1)}
. Consequently we have

\mathrm{K}({\mathbf{1}}_{\mathrm{KK}^{C_{p}}}[p^{-1}]\rtimes_{r}C_{p})\simeq KU[p^{-1}]^{\oplus p}\ .

(1.12)

This shows that the calculation based on (
1.10
) is compatible with the well-known general fact that for any finite group
G
we have an equivalence of
KU
-modules

\mathrm{K}({\mathbf{1}}_{\mathrm{KK}^{G}}\rtimes_{r}G)\simeq R(G)\simeq KU^{\bigoplus|\hat{G}|}\ .

This equivalence persists after inverting
p
and yields the equivalence (
1.12
) in the case
G=C_{p}
.

∎

Example 1.18
.

In this example we demonstrate the unitility of the results of the present paper for the explicit calculation of the
K
-theory of a crossed product of a rather complicated
A
in
\mathrm{KK}^{G}
.
If we knew how
A
is built from objects induced from smaller subgroups, then we could reduce the calculation of
\mathrm{K}(A\rtimes_{r}G)
to the calculation of crossed products for smaller subgroups. In contrast, multiplicative induction provides another source of objects of
\mathrm{KK}^{G}
which turn out to be much more difficult to understand.

On the level of
C^{*}
-algebras the multiplicative induction sends a
H
-
C^{*}
-algebra
B
for a subgroup
H
of
G
to the
G
-
C^{*}
-algebra given by the tensor product
B^{\otimes G/H}:=\bigotimes_{G/H}B
with an appropriate
G
-action (
2.33
). Thereby the choices of the minimal or maximal tensor products leads to two different versions of multiplicative induction.

In order to work on the level of homotopy theory in
2.21
we show that the multiplicative induction
descends to a symmetric monoidal functor

(-)^{\otimes G/H}:\mathrm{KK}^{H}\to\mathrm{KK}^{G}

which is not exact in general.

We consider the group
G:=C_{p^{k}}
for a prime
p
and
k\in{\rm Nature}\setminus\{0\}
. We further choose an integer
\ell
such that
p\not|\ell
and consider
B:={\mathbf{1}}_{\mathrm{KK}^{H}}/\ell
in
\mathrm{KK}^{H}
, where
H
is any subgroup of
C_{p^{k}}
for the moment. The assumption on
\ell
implies that
p
acts as an equivalence on
B
. Then by
2.23
it
also acts as an equivalence on
A:=B^{\otimes C_{p^{k}}/H}
. Therefore

1.16
applies to
A
.

If one likes to think in concrete terms, note that

A\simeq\mathrm{kk}^{C_{p^{k}}}(\underbrace{{\mathcal{O}}_{\ell+1}\otimes\dots\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{\ell+1}}_{C_{p^{k}}/H})

with the
C_{p^{k}}
action by permutations of tensor factors, and where
{\mathcal{O}}_{\ell+1}
is the Cuntz algebra.

The following calculates the second summand in the decomposition (
1.10
) as a
KU
-module.

Lemma 1.19

(Lemma
5.10
)
.

We have

\mathrm{K}(A\rtimes_{r}C_{p^{k}})[\xi^{-1}]\simeq R(C_{p^{k}})[\xi^{-1}]/\ell\simeq(KU/\ell)^{\oplus(p-1)p^{k}}\ .

Note that the result is independent of
H
.
The crucial fact enabling us to do this calculation is that the composition of the cofibre functor

\mathrm{Cof}^{C_{p^{k}}}
from (
1.7
) with the multiplicative induction functor preserves all colimits by
3.18
, so is in particular exact

In order to calculate the first summand in the decomposition (
1.10
) we restrict to the case
k=1
, the trivial subgroup
H
, and use (
1.11
).
We therefore must calculate the groups with
C_{p}
-action
\mathrm{K}_{*}(\widehat{\mathrm{Res}}^{C_{p}}(A))
and form coinvariants.

Lemma 1.20

(
5.11
)
.

We have

\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{BC_{p}}K_{*}(\widehat{\mathrm{Res}}^{C_{p}}(A))\simeq(KU/\ell)^{n_{0}(p)}\oplus(\Sigma KU/\ell)^{n_{1}(p)}

with

n_{i}(p):=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}\frac{2^{p-2}+\frac{(p+1)(p-1)}{2}}{p}&i=0\\
\frac{2^{p-2}-\frac{(p-1)^{2}}{2}}{p}&i=1\end{array}\right.

for
p>2
and

n_{i}(2):=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}1&i=0\\
0&i=1\end{array}\right.\ .

Combining these two results with (
1.10
) we get:

Corollary 1.21
.

For a prime
p
, an integer
\ell
with
p\not|\ell
, and
A:=({\mathbf{1}}_{\mathrm{KK}}/\ell)^{\otimes C_{p}}
in
\mathrm{KK}^{C_{p}}
we have

\mathrm{K}(A\rtimes_{r}C_{p})\simeq(KU/\ell)^{n_{0}(p)+(p-1)}\oplus(\Sigma KU/\ell)^{n_{1}(p)}\ .

We have the following table:

\begin{array}[]{|c||c|c|c|c|c|c|}\hline\cr p&2&3&5&7&11&\dots\\
\hline\cr n_{0}(p)+p-1&2&4&8&14&62&\dots\\
\hline\cr n_{1}(p)&0&0&0&2&42&\dots\\
\hline\cr\end{array}

The case
p=2
is due to
[
Izu
]
and the case
p=3
has previously been obtained using different methods by Kranz and Nishikawa.
4
4

4

These calculations are part of an ongoing joint project with J. Kranz and S. Nishikawa
with the main goal to settle the case where
p=\ell
, see
1.25
.

∎

We now come back to the question how one can get rid of the cofibre in the sequence (
1.7
), or equivalenty the third term in (
1.9
), by either adding additional assumptions on
A
or by
applying auxiliary operations.

By
4.3
.
1
we have a Bousfield localization

L_{\xi}:{\mathbf{Mod}}(R(G))\leftrightarrows L_{\xi}{\mathbf{Mod}}(R(G)):\mathrm{incl}

whose kernel consists precisely of the
\xi
-acyclic objects in
{\mathbf{Mod}}(R(G))
, i.e., the objects
C
satisfying
S_{\xi}(C)\simeq 0
. Recall that
\mathrm{K}(A\rtimes_{r}G)
has a canonical
R(G)
-module structure.
If we combine
Theorem

1.12
with
4.3
.
2
we get:

Corollary 1.22
.

We assume that
G
is finite. We have an equivalence

L_{\xi}(\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{G_{\mathcal{P}\mathrm{rp}}\mathbf{Orb}}\mathrm{K}^{G}_{A})\xrightarrow{\simeq}L_{\xi}(\mathrm{K}(A\rtimes_{r}G))

for every
A
in
\mathrm{KK}^{G}
.

Thus up to
\xi
-completion we can calculate
\mathrm{K}(A\rtimes_{r}G)
in terms of the
K
-theory of crossed products of
A
by proper subgroups of
G
and a colimit of a spectrum-valued functor over

G_{\mathcal{P}\mathrm{rp}}\mathbf{Orb}
.

If one is interested in
p
-torsion effects for a prime
p
, then it is natural to consider the
p
-completion
L_{p}
. We again consider the group
G=C_{p^{k}}
for
k
in
{\rm Nature}\setminus\{0\}
. For

A
in
\mathrm{KK}^{C_{p^{k}}}

we can indeed calculate
the
K
-theory spectrum
\mathrm{K}(A\rtimes_{r}C_{p^{k}})
after
p
-completion in terms of the spectrum
\mathrm{K}(\widehat{\mathrm{Res}}^{C_{p^{k}}}(A))
, i.e., the underlying
K
-theory spectrum of
A
with the induced
C_{p^{k}}
-action.

Theorem 1.23

(
Theorem

5.7
)
.

For any
A
in
\mathrm{KK}^{C_{p^{k}}}

the assembly map

\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{BC_{p^{k}}}\mathrm{K}(\widehat{\mathrm{Res}}^{C_{p^{k}}}(A))\to\mathrm{K}(A\rtimes_{r}C_{p^{k}})

(1.13)

becomes an equivalence after
p
-completion.

Example 1.24
.

If
\mathrm{K}(A)\simeq 0
, then
Theorem

1.23
implies that the
p
-completion of
\mathrm{K}(A\rtimes_{r}C_{p})
vanishes. By
4.3
.
2
this is equivalent to the assertion that
p
acts on
\mathrm{K}(A\rtimes_{r}C_{p})
as an equivalence, or that the groups
\mathrm{K}_{*}(A\rtimes_{r}C_{p})
are uniquely
p
-divisible. This fact has been observed in
[
Izu04
, Lemma 4.4]
with a different proof.
In
[
Izu04
]
,
[
BS17
]
the authors describe many examples of actions of
C_{p}
on a
C^{*}
-algebra
A

with
K(A)\simeq 0
such that
K_{*}(A\rtimes_{r}C_{p})
are non-trivial uniquely
p
-divisible groups.
This shows that the map in (
1.13
) is in general not an equivalence before
p
-completion.

∎

Example 1.25
.

By
2.21
for any
B
in
\mathrm{KK}
we can form the multiplicative induction
A:=B^{\otimes C_{p}}
in
\mathrm{KK}^{C_{p}}
. By
Theorem

1.23
the assembly map induces an equivalence

L_{p}(\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{BC_{p}}\mathrm{K}(\widehat{\mathrm{Res}}^{C_{p}}(A)))\to L_{p}(\mathrm{K}(A\rtimes_{r}C_{p}))\ .

(1.14)

If
B
is in the
\mathrm{UCT}
-class, then we have an equivalence

\mathrm{K}(\widehat{\mathrm{Res}}^{C_{p}}(A))\simeq\mathrm{K}(B)^{\otimes C_{p}}

in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BC_{p},{\mathbf{Mod}}(KU))
,
where the right-hand side has the induced action by permutation of tensor factors.
So the theorem reduces the calculation of the
p
-completion of
K(A\rtimes_{r}C_{p})
to the homotopy theoretic problem of calculating the
p
-completion of
\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{BC_{p}}\mathrm{K}(B)^{\otimes C_{p}}
. In general, to calculate the homotopy groups of this colimit is still a complicated task.

The following is the counterpart of the example discussed in
1.18
, but now in the case that
p|\ell
.
We consider
B:={\mathbf{1}}_{\mathrm{KK}}/\ell
which belongs to the
\mathrm{UCT}
-class. A concrete realization of
A
is then given in terms of the Cuntz algebra by

A\simeq\mathrm{kk}^{C_{p}}(\underbrace{{\mathcal{O}}_{\ell+1}\otimes\dots\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{\ell+1}}_{C_{p}})\ .

In this case the homotopy groups of
\mathrm{K}(A\rtimes_{r}C_{p})
are known for
p=2
by
[
Izu
]

\mathrm{K}_{*}(({\mathbf{1}}_{\mathrm{KK}}/2)^{\otimes C_{2}}\rtimes_{r}C_{2})\simeq\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}{\mathbb{Z}}/2\ell{\mathbb{Z}}\oplus{\mathbb{Z}}/\frac{\ell}{2}{\mathbb{Z}}&*=0\\
0&*=1\end{array}\right.

and

p=3
by Kranz and Nishikawa (yet unpublished).
As far as we know, the case
p\geq 5
is still an open problem.

∎

We finally discuss conditions under which the assembly map (
1.13
) is an equivalence even before
p
-completion. We concentrate on the group
C_{p}
.
We let
{\mathbf{G}}(p)
denote the localizing subcategory of
\mathrm{KK}^{C_{p}}
generated by objects of the following form:

1.

\mathrm{Res}_{C_{p}}(A^{\prime})
for some
A^{\prime}
in
\mathrm{KK}
such that
\mathrm{K}_{*}(A^{\prime})
is annihilated by
p^{n}
for some
n
in
{\rm Nature}
.

2.

{\mathtt{Ind}}^{C_{p}}(A^{\prime\prime})
for some
A^{\prime\prime}
in
\mathrm{KK}
.

Proposition 1.26

(
5.9
)
.

If
A
is in
{\mathbf{G}}(p)
, then
the assembly map is an equivalence

\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{BC_{p}}\mathrm{K}(A)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}\mathrm{K}(A\rtimes_{r}C_{p})

(1.15)

Example 1.27
.

We consider
B:={\mathbf{1}}_{\mathrm{KK}}/p
and
A:=B^{\otimes C_{p}}
.

Lemma 1.28

(
5.12
)
.

We have
A\in{\mathbf{G}}(p)
.

By
1.26

we conclude

\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{BC_{p}}\mathrm{K}(\widehat{\mathrm{Res}}^{C_{p}}(({\mathbf{1}}_{\mathrm{KK}}/p)^{\otimes C_{p}}))\simeq\mathrm{K}(({\mathbf{1}}_{\mathrm{KK}}/p)^{\otimes C_{p}}\rtimes_{r}C_{p})\ ,

i.e. there is no need of
p
-completion in order to calculate this crossed product.
∎

2
Some homotopy theory

2.1
Equivariant KK-theory

Equivariant group-valued
KK
-theory was introduced in
[
Kas88
]
. Following
[
Hig87
]
,
[
Tho98
]
,
[
Mey00
]
it should be considered as an additive category receiving
a functor from
G
-
C^{*}
-algebras which can be characterized by a universal property. By
[
MN06
]
the additive equivariant
KK
-category has a canonical structure of a triangulated category.
In order to perform homotopy theoretic constructions, e.g. homotopy colimits over group actions on objects in this category, we will work with the
\infty
-categorical enhancement of equivariant
KK
-theory. It is given by a functor from
G
-
C^{*}
-algebras to
a stable
\infty
-category which is again characterized a universal property.
The classical picture can be recovered by going over to the homotopy category.
In this subsection we recall the main features of this
\infty
-categorical version of
equivariant
KK
-theory. In the case of a discrete group
G
(which is the only relevant case for the applications in the present paper) a reference is
[
BELb
]
.

5
5

5

The general case of second countable locally compact groups was the topic of a course taught in Regensburg 2023 whose notes are in preparation.

Let
G
be a second countable locally compact group. We then consider the category of
G
-
C^{*}
-algebras
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
.
Its objects are
C^{*}
-algebras with an action of
G
by automorphisms such that
the maps
G\to A
,
g\mapsto ga
, are
continuous for all
a
in
A
. The morphisms of
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
are equivariant homomorphisms.

In the following we will use the language of
\infty
-categories as developed in
[
Lur09
]

[
Lur
]
. There exists a presentable stable
\infty
-category

\mathrm{KK}^{G}
and a functor

\mathrm{kk}^{G}:GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\to\mathrm{KK}^{G}

(2.1)

which
is characterized by the following universal property: For any cocomplete stable
\infty
-category
{\mathcal{C}}

the precomposition by
\mathrm{kk}^{G}
induces an equivalence

{\mathbf{Fun}}^{\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}}(\mathrm{KK}^{G},{\mathcal{C}})\xrightarrow{\simeq}{\mathbf{Fun}}^{h,K_{G},\mathrm{se},\mathrm{sfin}}(GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}},{\mathcal{C}})

(2.2)

of functor categories.
Here the superscripts indicate full subcategories of functors satisfying conditions. The superscript
\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}
stands for colimit-preserving functors. The superscripts

h
,
K_{G}
,
\mathrm{se}
and
\mathrm{sfin}
stand for the conditions of homotopy invariance,
K_{G}
-stability, semi-exactness and
s
-finitariness which will be explained in the following. Consider a functor
F
on
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
:

1.

It is homotopy invariant if it sends the canonical homomorphisms
A\to C([0,1],A)
,
a\mapsto{\mathtt{const}}_{a}
, to equivalences for all
A
in
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
.

2.

It is
K_{G}
-stable if it sends
K_{G}
-equivalences to equivalences. Here a morphism
f:A\to B
is a
K_{G}
-equivalence if
f\otimes{\mathtt{id}}_{G}:A\otimes K_{G}\to B\otimes K_{G}
is a homotopy equivalence, where
K_{G}
is the algebra of compact operators on the
G
-Hilbert space
L^{2}(G)\otimes L^{2}({\rm Nature})
with
G
-action given by the left-regular representation on
L^{2}(G)
.

3.

It is semiexact if it sends semisplit exact sequences
0\to A\to B\to C\to 0
in
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
to fibre sequences. The condition of being semisplit means that the map
B\to C
admits an equivariant completely positive contractive right inverse.

4.

It is
s
-finitary if the canonical morphisms
\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{A^{\prime}\subseteq_{\mathrm{sep}}A}F(A^{\prime})\to F(A)

are equivalences for all
A
in
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
. Here
A^{\prime}\subseteq_{\mathrm{sep}}A
is the poset of
G
-invariant separable subalgebras
of
A
.

The existence of the functor (
2.1
) is shown by providing a construction
whose main idea is to force the desired universal properties using Dwyer-Kan localizations and
{\mathtt{Ind}}
-completion.

The minimal tensor product equips
the category
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
with a symmetric monoidal structure
\otimes
. We will denote the tensor unit by
{\mathbf{1}}_{\mathrm{KK}^{G}}
. Using the symmetric monoidal version of Dwyer-Kan localizations
[
Hin16
]
and
{\mathtt{Ind}}
-completion
the category
\mathrm{KK}^{G}
acquires a presentably symmetric monoidal structure
and functor
\mathrm{kk}^{G}
has a symmetric monoidal refinement characterized by the following universal property: For every presentably symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category
{\mathcal{C}}
the precomposition by
\mathrm{kk}^{G}
induces an equivalence

{\mathbf{Fun}}_{\otimes/\mathrm{lax}}^{\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}}(\mathrm{KK}^{G},{\mathcal{C}})\xrightarrow{\simeq}{\mathbf{Fun}}_{\otimes/\mathrm{lax}}^{h,K_{G},\mathrm{se},\mathrm{sfin}}(GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}},{\mathcal{C}})\ ,

(2.3)

where the subscripts
\otimes
or
\mathrm{lax}
stand for symmetric monoidal or lax symmetric monoidal functors, respectively.
There is an analogous statement for the maximal tensor product on
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
.

By the universal property of
\mathrm{kk}^{G}
we get factorizations

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 26.53954pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-26.53954pt\raise 108.61334pt\raise-23.68417pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.83948pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\mathrm{kk}^{G}_{h,K_{G}}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 91.21986pt\raise-26.72365pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise-32.17834pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 62.93828pt\raise-32.17834pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathrm{KK}^{G}}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 157.39467pt\raise-32.17834pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\ ,

(2.4)

where
L_{h}
and
L_{K_{G}}
are the Dwyer-Kan localizations inverting homotopy equivalences and
K_{G}
-equivalences.

A main feature of equivariant
KK
-theory is the presence of a variety of group-change functors and various adjunctions between them. On the level of triangulated categories they are reviewed in
[
MN06
]
.
In the following we list their refinements to the
\infty
-categorical level. Thereby we will provide sufficient data to fix the functors and adjunctions essentially uniquely.

If
H\to G
is a homomorphism, then we have a restriction functor

\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{H}:GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\to HC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}

(2.5)

which restricts the
G
-action to a
H
-action along this homomorpism.

Furthermore, for a closed subgroup
H
of
G
have an induction functor

{\mathtt{Ind}}_{H}^{G}:HC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\to GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\ .

(2.6)

It sends
A
on
HC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
to the closure in
C_{b}(G,A)
(the bounded continuous functions on
G
with values in
A
with the supremum norm) of the subalgebra of functions
f

which are
H
-invariant in the sense that
f(gh)=hf(g)
for all
h
in
H
, and whose support projects to a compact subset of
G/H
. The group
G
acts on this subalgebra by the left-regular representation

(g^{\prime},f)\mapsto(g\mapsto f(g^{\prime,-1}g))
.

Both functors descend to equivariant
KK
-theory, i.e., we have canonical commutative squares

3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 77.71031pt\raise-23.26608pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 27.13089pt\raise-25.45833pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.71446pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{{\mathtt{Ind}}_{H}^{G}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 63.56952pt\raise-32.17834pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 63.56952pt\raise-32.17834pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathrm{KK}^{G}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\ .

We have a symmetric monoidal functor

\widehat{\mathrm{Res}}^{G}:GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\to{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h})

which interprets
G
-
C^{*}
-algebra as an object of
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h}
with the induced
G
-action.
This functor also descends to
KK
-theory in the sense that we have a canonical commutative square

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 26.53954pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-26.53954pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 29.85165pt\raise 6.61111pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-3.61111pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\mathrm{Res}}^{G}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 50.53954pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-16.22806pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-3.21724pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\mathrm{kk}^{G}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-23.54387pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 50.53954pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 96.7613pt\raise-16.22806pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.90001pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\mathrm{kk}_{h}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 96.7613pt\raise-24.45613pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-14.1408pt\raise-32.45613pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathrm{KK}^{G}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 29.98418pt\raise-25.84502pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-3.61111pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\mathrm{Res}}^{G}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 63.20334pt\raise-32.45613pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 63.20334pt\raise-32.45613pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,\mathrm{KK})}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\ .

(2.7)

Let
UM(A)
denote the unitary group of the multiplier algebra of
A
equipped with the strict topology.

Lemma 2.1
.

If
the
G
-action on
A
is induced by conjugation with a continuous homomorphism
\rho:G\to UM(A)
,
then we have an equivalence

\mathrm{kk}^{G}(A)\simeq\mathrm{Res}_{G}\mathrm{Res}^{G}(\mathrm{kk}^{G}(A))

(2.8)

in
\mathrm{KK}^{G}
.

Proof.

The equivalence in (
2.8
) is induced by the cocycle isomorphism

({\mathtt{id}}_{A},\rho):\mathrm{Res}_{G}(\mathrm{Res}^{G}(A))\to A\ .

This is not simply a morphism in
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
.
But cocycle morphisms can naturally be interpreted as morphisms in
L_{K_{G}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h}
and therefore also give rise to morphisms in
\mathrm{KK}^{G}
via the factorization (
2.4
).
∎

Remark 2.2
.

Under the assumptions of
2.1

we can conclude that the
G
-action on
\mathrm{kk}(A)
in
\mathrm{KK}
induced by functoriality
is trivial
since this
G
-object can be written formally as

\widehat{\mathrm{Res}}^{G}(\mathrm{kk}^{G}(A))\simeq\widehat{\mathrm{Res}}^{G}\mathrm{Res}_{G}\mathrm{Res}^{G}(\mathrm{kk}^{G}(A))\ ,

and the right-hand side obviously has the trivial
G
-action.
This refines the classical fact that the action of a group on a
C^{*}
-algebra by inner automorphisms induces
the trivial action on
K
-theory groups.
∎

If
H
is closed and open in
G
, then we have an adjunction

{\mathtt{Ind}}^{G}_{H}:\mathrm{KK}^{H}\leftrightarrows\mathrm{KK}^{G}:\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{H}

(2.9)

whose unit
A\mapsto\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{H}({\mathtt{Ind}}^{G}_{H}(A))
is already defined on the level of algebras and sends
a
in
A
to the function

g\mapsto\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}g^{-1}a&g\in H\\
0&g\not\in H\end{array}\right.\ .

This function is indeed continuous by the openess assumption on
H
.
If
G/H
is in addition finite, then we also have an adjunction

\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{H}:\mathrm{KK}^{G}\leftrightarrows\mathrm{KK}^{H}:{\mathtt{Ind}}^{G}_{H}

(2.10)

whose unit
A\to{\mathtt{Ind}}_{H}^{G}(\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{H}(A))
and counit

\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{H}({\mathtt{Ind}}^{G}_{H}(A))\to A
are already defined on the level of algebras and are given by
the maps
a\mapsto(g\mapsto g^{-1}a)
and the
evaluation
f\mapsto f(e)
, respectively.

For
A
in
\mathrm{KK}^{H}
and
B
in
\mathrm{KK}^{G}
we have a bi-natural projection formula

{\mathtt{Ind}}_{H}^{G}(A)\otimes B\simeq{\mathtt{Ind}}_{H}^{G}(A\otimes\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{H}(B))

(2.11)

which follows from the analoguous isomorphism

f\otimes b\mapsto(g\mapsto f(g)\otimes g^{-1}b)
on the level of
C^{*}
-algebras.

For
A
in
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
we can form the convolution
*
-algebra
C_{c}(G,A)
. It is a pre-
C^{*}
-algebra in the sense that all elements have a finite maximal norm. The completion of
C_{c}(G,A)
is the maximal crossed product
A\rtimes_{\max}G
. If
A\to B(H)
is a faithful representation of
A
on some Hilbert space
H
, then
the representation of
A\rtimes_{\max}G

on
L^{2}(G,H)
induces the reduced norm on
A\rtimes_{\max}G
. The completion of
A\rtimes_{\max}G
with respect to the reduced norm is called the reduced crossed product
A\rtimes_{r}G
.

The crossed product functors

-\rtimes_{r}G,-\rtimes_{\max}G:GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\to C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}

both descends to equivariant
KK
-theory, i.e., we have canonical commutative squares

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 26.53954pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-26.53954pt\raise 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 50.53954pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise
