106.62793pt\raise-27.72476pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.8264pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{q}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 121.54529pt\raise-32.91226pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 121.54529pt\raise-32.91226pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathrm{ee}^{G}_{\oplus}(A^{\infty}_{r})}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\ ,

where lower horizontal sequence is part of a fibre sequence.

2.37
now implies that
\mathrm{ee}^{G}_{\oplus}(A)
is a universal phantom retract of

\mathrm{ee}^{G}_{\oplus}(A^{\infty}_{r})
. But by
2.42
the latter object belongs to

{\mathtt{Ind}}^{G}(\mathrm{E}_{\oplus})
.
∎

Proposition 2.44

(
1.4
)
.

If
G
is finite and
A
is a
G
-
C^{*}
-algebra with the Rokhlin property, then the assembly map is an equivalence

\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{BG}K(A)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}K(A\rtimes_{r}G)\ .

Proof.

We extend the natural transformation (
2.32
) to a cofibre sequence of functors with cofibre denoted by
\mathrm{Cof}
.
We have equivalences

\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{BG}\mathrm{K}(\widehat{\mathrm{Res}}^{G}(\mathrm{kk}^{G}(-)))\simeq\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{BG}K(-)\ ,\quad\mathrm{K}(\mathrm{kk}^{G}(-)\rtimes_{r}G)\simeq K(-\rtimes_{r}G)

(2.42)

of functors from
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
to

{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,{\mathbf{Mod}}(KU))
. These functors are homotopy invariant,
G
-stable, exact,
s
-finitary
and preserve countable sums. By (
2.21
) they therefore factorize over

\mathrm{ee}^{G}_{\oplus}
. We therefore also have a colimit preserving factorization
\mathrm{Cof}
through colimit preserving functors

\mathrm{KK}^{G}\xrightarrow{c^{G}}\mathrm{E}_{\oplus}^{G}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{Cof}^{E}}{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,{\mathbf{Mod}}(KU))\ .

Since
\langle c^{G}(\langle{\mathtt{Ind}}^{G}(\mathrm{KK})\rangle)\rangle=\langle{\mathtt{Ind}}^{G}(\mathrm{E}_{\oplus})\rangle

it follows from
1.6
that
\mathrm{Cof}^{E}
annihilates the localizing subcategory generated by
{\mathtt{Ind}}^{G}(\mathrm{E}_{\oplus})
. Since
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,{\mathbf{Mod}}(KU))
is compactly generated we can conclude by
2.36
that

\mathrm{Cof}^{E}
annihilates
\overline{\langle{\mathtt{Ind}}^{G}(\mathrm{E}_{\oplus})\rangle}
. Thus

\mathrm{Cof}(\mathrm{kk}^{G}(A))\simeq\mathrm{Cof}^{E}(\mathrm{ee}_{\oplus}^{G}(A))\simeq 0

by
2.43
and therefore

\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{BG}\mathrm{K}(\widehat{\mathrm{Res}}^{G}(\mathrm{kk}^{G}(A)))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}\mathrm{K}(\mathrm{kk}^{G}(A)\rtimes_{r}G)\ .

In view of the equivalences (
2.42
) this is equivalent to the assertion of
1.4
.
∎

2.5
Projectivity

We assume that
G
is discrete and consider
M
in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,{\mathbf{Mod}}(KU))
.
For example,
M
could be the
K
-theory spectrum
K(A)
with the
G
-action induced by functoriality of a
G
-
C^{*}
-algebra
A
. As we have seen above the calculation of the
K
-theory groups of crossed products is often reduced to the calculation of

\pi_{*}(\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{BG}M)
. We want to understand these homotopy groups in terms of
the homotopy groups
\pi_{*}(M)
with the
G
-action induced by functoriality.
In general we have a spectral sequence with second term

E^{2}_{p,q}\cong H_{p}(G,\pi_{q}(M))

converging to
\pi_{*}(\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{BG}M)
.
But even for simple groups like
G=C_{p}
it turns out to be too complicated to use this for explicit calculations.

In this section we discuss the condition of projectivity of
M
we simplifies this calculation considerably.
If
M
is projective, then this spectral sequence degenerates since
E^{2}_{p,q}=0
for
p\not=0
and
the groups
\pi_{*}(\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{BG}M)
have a simple algebraic description.

We then discuss conditions on
C^{*}
-algebras
A
(or objects
A
in
\mathrm{KK}^{G}
) which imply that
K(A)
(or
\mathrm{K}(\widehat{\mathrm{Res}}^{G}(A))
) is projective.

Definition 2.45
.

1.

M
is called free if it is equivalent to
\bigoplus_{G}N
with the
G
-action by left translations on the index set for some
N
in
{\mathbf{Mod}}(KU)
.

2.

M
is called projective if it is a retract of a free object.

Lemma 2.46
.

1.

If
M
is projective, then we have an isomorphism

\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{BG}\pi_{*}(M)\cong\pi_{*}(\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{BG}M)\ .

(2.43)

2.

If
G
is finite and
M
is projective, then we have an equivalence (or isomorphism, respectively)

\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{BG}M\simeq\operatorname*{\mathtt{lim}}_{BG}M\ ,\quad\pi_{*}(\operatorname*{\mathtt{lim}}_{BG}M)\cong\operatorname*{\mathtt{lim}}_{BG}\pi_{*}(M)\ .

(2.44)

Proof.

We believe that
the assertions of this lemma are well-known, but let us add the argument for completeness.
For any cocomplete
\infty
-category
{\mathcal{C}}
we have adjunctions

\bigoplus_{G}:{\mathcal{C}}\leftrightarrows{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,{\mathcal{C}}):\mathrm{Res}^{G}\ ,\quad\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{BG}:{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,{\mathcal{C}})\leftrightarrows{\mathcal{C}}:\mathrm{Res}_{G}\ ,

(2.45)

where
\mathrm{Res}^{G}
forgets the
G
-action and
\mathrm{Res}_{G}
introduces the trivial
G
-action.
The second adjunction is the definition of the colimit, and the first is the precise description of the functor
\bigoplus_{G}
. We have an equivalence

\mathrm{Res}^{G}\circ\mathrm{Res}_{G}\simeq{\mathtt{id}}

(2.46)

which induces the equivalence of adjoints

\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{BG}\bigoplus_{G}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{veervwercecsfv}}}{{\simeq}}\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{BG}\bigoplus_{G}\mathrm{Res}^{G}\mathrm{Res}_{G}\xrightarrow{units,\simeq}{\mathtt{id}}_{{\mathcal{C}}}\ ,

(2.47)

where the arrow marked with
units
is given by the composition of the units of the two adjunctions. We apply this equivalence (
2.47
) to the natural transformation

\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{BG}\pi_{*}\to\pi_{*}\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{BG}

(2.48)

of functors from
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,{\mathbf{Mod}}(KU))
to
{\mathbf{Ab}}^{{\mathbb{Z}}}
, precomposed with
\bigoplus_{G}
, and get the commutative square

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 42.13667pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-42.13667pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{BG}\pi_{*}(\bigoplus_{G}(-))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 50.00473pt\raise 5.43056pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.43056pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{!}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 66.13667pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-16.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\cong}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-24.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 66.13667pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\pi_{*}(\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{BG}\bigoplus_{G}(-))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 108.27335pt\raise-16.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\cong}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 108.27335pt\raise-24.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-13.70432pt\raise-32.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\pi_{*}(-)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 1.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 1.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}}}{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 1.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}}}{\hbox{\kern 94.56903pt\raise-32.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\pi_{*}(-)}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\ .

(2.49)

This shows that the marked transformation is an isomorphism.
It provides the isomorphism (
2.43
) for free objects
M
.
Since
a retract of an isomorphism is again an isomorphism
we conclude that the natural transformation (
2.48
) more generally induces the isomorphism (
2.43
) for projective
M
.

If
{\mathcal{C}}
is also complete, then we have
adjunctions

\mathrm{Res}^{G}:{\mathcal{C}}\leftrightarrows{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,{\mathcal{C}}):\prod_{G}\ ,\quad\mathrm{Res}_{G}:{\mathcal{C}}\leftrightarrows{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,{\mathcal{C}}):\operatorname*{\mathtt{lim}}_{BG}\ .

(2.50)

The analogue of (
2.47
) is an equivalence

{\mathtt{id}}_{{\mathcal{C}}}\xrightarrow{\simeq}\operatorname*{\mathtt{lim}}_{BG}\prod_{G}\ .

(2.51)

We now assume that
{\mathcal{C}}
is additive.
We have a natural transformation
{\mathtt{id}}_{{\mathcal{C}}}\to\mathrm{Res}^{G}\prod_{G}
which sends
M
to the map

(f_{g})_{g\in G}:M\to\prod_{G}M
given by

f_{g}:=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}{\mathtt{id}}_{M}&g=e\\
0&g\not=e\end{array}\right.\ .

If
G

is finite, then the adjoint of this transformation is an equivalence

\mathrm{Nm}^{\mathrm{Res}^{G}}:\bigoplus_{G}\xrightarrow{\simeq}\prod_{G}\ .

(2.52)

To see this we observe that after applying
\mathrm{Res}^{G}
we get the canonical equivalence between a the coproduct and the product in an additive category, and we use that
\mathrm{Res}^{G}
is conservative.

Remark 2.47
.

(\mathrm{Res}^{G},\mathrm{Nm}^{\mathrm{Res}^{G}})
is a tensor iso-normed functor in the sense of
[
CSY22
, Def. 2.3.1]
.
∎

The analog of the argument above now shows that the natural transformation

\pi_{*}\operatorname*{\mathtt{lim}}_{BG}\to\operatorname*{\mathtt{lim}}_{BG}\pi_{*}

induces an isomorphism (
2.44
)
on projective objects in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,{\mathbf{Mod}}(KU))
.

In order to see the first equivalence in (
2.44
) we
use the norm map

\mathrm{Nm}^{\mathrm{Res}_{G}}:\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{BG}\to\operatorname*{\mathtt{lim}}_{BG}

(2.53)

between functors from
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,{\mathcal{C}})
to
{\mathcal{C}}

which again exists for finite
G
and additive
{\mathcal{C}}
.

We claim that it induces and equivalence when applied to free objects.
In order see this we could cite
[
NS18
, Lemma 1.3.8]
. Alternatively, we could use
[
HL
, 4.2.4]
in order to see that the composition

\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{BG}\circ\bigoplus_{G}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{Nm}^{\mathrm{Res}_{G}}\bigoplus_{G}}\operatorname*{\mathtt{lim}}_{BG}\circ\bigoplus_{G}\xrightarrow{\operatorname*{\mathtt{lim}}_{BG}\mathrm{Nm}^{\mathrm{Res}^{G}}}\operatorname*{\mathtt{lim}}_{BG}\prod_{G}\ ,

under the equivalences (
2.51
) and (
2.47
),
is equivalent to the norm of the identity functor of
{\mathcal{C}}
.
Since
\mathrm{Nm}^{\mathrm{Res}^{G}}
from (
2.52
) is known to be an equivalence
we see that
\mathrm{Nm}^{\mathrm{Res}_{G}}\bigoplus_{G}

is an equivalence, too.
The claim implies that the norm is also an equivalence on projective objects.
∎

Remark 2.48
.

If
{\mathcal{C}}
is stable, then the norm map (
2.53
) is a natural transformation between finite limit preserving functors. It follows that the equivalence

\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{BG}M\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}\operatorname*{\mathtt{lim}}_{BG}M

persists to compact objects
M
in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,{\mathcal{C}})
, i.e. objects which can presented as finite colimits of projectives.
∎

We assume that
G
is finite and consider
M
in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,{\mathbf{Mod}}(KU))
.

Lemma 2.49
.

If
|G|
acts as an equivalence on
M
, then
M
is projective.

Proof.

We have the unit and the counit maps

\bigoplus_{G}\mathrm{Res}^{G}M\to M\ ,\quad M\to\prod_{G}\mathrm{Res}^{G}M

of the first adjunctions in (
2.45
) and (
2.50
), respectively.
One can check that the composition

M\to\prod_{G}\mathrm{Res}^{G}M\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{ewrogpwefrefewfr}}}{{\simeq}}\bigoplus_{G}\mathrm{Res}^{G}M\to M

(2.54)

is given by multiplication by
|G|
. Since
|G|
acts as an equivalence on
M
the composition (
2.54
) exhibits
M
as a retract of the free object
\bigoplus_{G}\mathrm{Res}^{G}M
.
∎

Remark 2.50
.

We note that
1.8
and
2.49
can be considered as instances of
the higher Maschke Theorem as stated in
[
CSY22
, Thm. 2.4.4]

Example 2.51
.

Assume that
G
is finite and consider the UHF algebra

M_{|G|^{\infty}}:=\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{n\in{\rm Nature}}\bigotimes_{i=0}^{n}{\mathtt{Mat}}_{|G|}({\mathbb{C}})

(2.55)

in
C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
. For any
G
-
C^{*}
-algebra
A
we have an equivalence

\mathrm{kk}^{G}(A\otimes M_{|G|^{\infty}})\simeq\mathrm{kk}^{G}(A)[|G|^{-1}]\ .

If
A
is a
G
-
C^{*}
-algebra which tensorially absorbs
M_{|G|^{\infty}}
, i.e.,

A\cong A\otimes M_{|G|^{\infty}}
, then also
\mathrm{kk}^{G}(A)\simeq\mathrm{kk}^{G}(A)[|G|^{-1}]
.

Corollary 2.52
.

If
A
tensorially absorbs
M_{|G|^{\infty}}
, then
K(A)
satisfies the assumption of
2.49
and is therefore projective in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,{\mathbf{Mod}}(KU))
.

Assume now that
A
has the trivial
G
-action and tensorially absorbs
M_{|G|^{\infty}}
.
Then on the one hand we have

\pi_{*}(\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{BG}K(A))\cong\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{BG}K_{*}(A)\cong K_{*}(A)\ .

On the other hand, since
K(C_{r}^{*}(G))\simeq\bigoplus_{|\hat{G}|}KU
and
A\rtimes_{r}G\cong A\otimes C_{r}^{*}(G)
(see (
2.15
) for the
KK
-theory level version)
we have

K_{*}(A\rtimes_{r}G)\cong\bigoplus_{|\hat{G}|}K_{*}(A)\ .

If
G
is non-trivial, then the map

\pi_{*}(\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{BG}K(A))\to K_{*}(A\rtimes_{r}G)

is not an isomorphism.
This shows that condition

1.8
.
1
can not be replaced by the weaker condition
that
|G|
acts as an equivalence.
8
8

8

contrary to a wrong statement in a previous version of this paper

∎

Remark 2.53
.

Assume that
A
is a unital Kirchberg algebra which absorbs
M_{|G|^{\infty}}
and assume that

G\to\mathrm{KK}_{0}(A,A)^{-1}
is a homomorphism fixing the class of the identity. Then by
[
BS17
, Rem. 2.12]
this homomorphism
comes from a refinement of
A
to a
G
-
C^{*}
-algebra with the Rokhlin property.
By
2.49
we know that
K(A)
is a projective object in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,{\mathbf{Mod}}(KU))
.
We can therefore
deduce from

1.4
and (
2.43
) that

\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{BG}K(A)\simeq K(A\rtimes_{r}G)\ ,\qquad\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{BG}K_{*}(A)\simeq K_{*}(A\rtimes_{r}G)\ .

(2.56)

∎

The following is consequence of
2.43
.

Corollary 2.54
.

Assume that
G
is finite.
If
A
has the Rokhlin property, then
K(A)
is a universal strong phantom retract of a free object.

Proof.

We use that the functor
\mathrm{K}^{E}\circ\widehat{\mathrm{Res}}^{G}
preserves colimits and therefore universal strong phantom retracts. It furthermore
sends the objects of
{\mathtt{Ind}}^{G}(\mathrm{E}_{\oplus})
to free objects.
By
2.43
we conclude that
\mathrm{K}^{E}(\widehat{\mathrm{Res}}^{G}(\mathrm{ee}^{G}_{\oplus}(A)))\simeq K(A)
is a universal strong phantom retract of a free object.
∎

Note that
2.54
does not imply that the object
K(A)
is a projective object
in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,{\mathbf{Mod}}(KU))
since in this category not every
universal strong phantom retract is a retract.
But we can obtain projectivity under additional assumptions, different from the property that
|G|
acts as an equivalence.

Let
A
be in
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
.

Proposition 2.55
.

Assume that
G
is finite. If
A
is simple, has the Rokhlin property, and

K(A)
is compact
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,{\mathbf{Mod}}(KU))
,
then
K(A)
is projective.

Proof.

By Lemma (
2.42
) and the observations made in
1.3
we have an equivalence

K(A^{\infty}_{r})\simeq\bigoplus_{G}K(A^{\infty,G}_{r})
in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,{\mathbf{Mod}}(KU))
, where the
G
-action on the sum is given by left-translations
on the index set.
The idea of the proof of
2.55
is to present
K(A)
as a retract of the free object
K(A^{\infty}_{r})
.

By the proof of
2.43
we have a fibre sequence

F\xrightarrow{\iota}\mathrm{ee}_{\oplus}^{G}(A)\to\mathrm{ee}_{\oplus}^{G}(A_{r}^{\infty})

in
\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\oplus}
, where
\iota
is a universal strong phantom morphism.
Since
\mathrm{K}^{E}\circ\widehat{\mathrm{Res}}^{G}
preserves colimits and therefore preserves universal strong phantom morphism
9
9

9

It is also a right-adjoint and therefore preserves strong phantoms.
we conclude that

\mathrm{K}^{E}(\widehat{\mathrm{Res}}^{G}(\iota)):\mathrm{K}^{E}(\widehat{\mathrm{Res}}^{G}(F))\to K(A)
is a universal strong phantom morphism.

By assumption
K(A)
is a compact object on
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,{\mathbf{Mod}}(KU))
.
We use simpleness of
A
to the effect that the non-equivariant homomorphism
A\to A^{\infty}_{r}
,
a\mapsto p_{e}a^{\prime}
is injective and thus identifies
A
with the corner
p_{e}A^{\infty}_{r}
. In view of the proof of
2.42
we conclude that
K(A^{\infty}_{r})\simeq\bigoplus_{G}\mathrm{Res}^{G}(K(A))
is also compact in

{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,{\mathbf{Mod}}(KU))
. This implies that the left entry of the fibre sequence

K^{E}(\widehat{\mathrm{Res}}^{G}(F))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{K}^{E}(\widehat{\mathrm{Res}}^{G}(\iota))}}{{\to}}K(A)\to K(A^{\infty}_{r})

(2.57)

is also compact. But then
\mathrm{K}^{E}(\widehat{\mathrm{Res}}^{G}(\iota))\simeq 0

since it is a strong phantom morphism from a compact object.
We conclude that the fibre sequence (
2.57
) splits and

K(A^{\infty}_{r})\simeq K(A)\oplus\Sigma\mathrm{K}^{E}(\widehat{\mathrm{Res}}^{G}(F))

in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,{\mathbf{Mod}}(KU))
.
∎

Remark 2.56
.

In the statement of
2.55
we could replace the simpleness of
A
with the assumption that
A\to A^{\infty}_{r}p_{e}
is injective, or simply with the assumption that
K(A^{\infty}_{r})
is a compact object in

{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,{\mathbf{Mod}}(KU))
. But note that these assumptions do not only depend on
A
but also on the choice of the additional
data given by the Rokhlin property.

Note that the compactness of

K(A)
in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,{\mathbf{Mod}}(KU))
is a strong assumption, much stronger than compactness of
\mathrm{Res}^{G}(K(A))
in
{\mathbf{Mod}}(KU)
.

∎

Combining
2.55
with
1.4
,
(
2.43
) and (
2.44
) we conclude:

Corollary 2.57
.

Under the assumption of
2.55
we have isomorphisms

\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{BG}K_{*}(A)\cong K_{*}(A\rtimes_{r}G)\cong\operatorname*{\mathtt{lim}}_{BG}K_{*}(A)\ .

(2.58)

Remark 2.58
.

In this remark we explain how
2.57
is related with the results of
[
Izu04
]
.
Let
A
be a
G
-
C^{*}
-algebra for a finite group
G
and let
A^{G}
be the subalgebra of
G
-fixed points.
By
[
Rie80
, Prop. 4.3]
we have an isomorphism of
C^{*}
-algebras

(A\otimes K(L^{2}(G))^{G}\cong A\rtimes_{r}G\ .

(2.59)

By
[
Izu04
, Lemma. 3.11]
, if
A
is unital and has the Rokhlin property,
then
A
is stable, i.e., we have an isomorphism
A\cong A\otimes K(L^{2}(G))
.
We conclude under these assumptions that

A^{G}\cong A\rtimes_{r}G\ .

(2.60)

If
A
is in addition simple, then
[
Izu04
, Thm. 3.13]
states that the inclusion
A^{G}\to A
induces an isomorphism

K_{*}(A^{G})\cong\operatorname*{\mathtt{lim}}_{BG}K_{*}(A)\ .

(2.61)

If we combine (
2.60
), (
2.61
), then we can conclude that if
A
is simple, unital, and satisfies the Rokhlin property, then

K_{*}(A\rtimes_{r}G)\cong\operatorname*{\mathtt{lim}}_{BG}K_{*}(A)

(2.62)

We thus get the second isomorphism in the statement of
2.57
.

Turning things around, up to the additional compactness assumption on
K(A)
,

2.57
provides a new proof of
[
Izu04
, Thm. 3.13]
, which seems to be very different from Izumi’s argument.

∎

3
The K-theory functor on the orbit category

3.1
A KK-valued functor on the orbit category

Let
G
be a second countable locally compact group.
A
G
-orbit is a locally compact topological
G
-space
which is isomorphic to a
G
-space of the form
G/H
for some closed subgroup
H
of
G
.
By
G\mathbf{Orb}
we denote the
\infty
-category presented by the full topologically enriched subcategory of
G{\mathbf{Top}}
on
G
-orbits. If
G
is discrete, then
G\mathbf{Orb}
is an ordinary category.

By
G\mathbf{Orb}^{{\mathtt{comp}}}
or
G\mathbf{Orb}^{\mathrm{fin}}
we denote the full subcategories of
G\mathbf{Orb}
on
G
-orbits which are compact or finite, respectively. For a family of subgroups
{\mathcal{F}}
, i.e.,
a set of closed subgroups of
G
which is closed under conjugation and taking subgroups, we let
G_{{\mathcal{F}}}\mathbf{Orb}
denote the full subcategory of
G\mathbf{Orb}
on
G
-orbits isomorphic to
G/H
with
H
in
{\mathcal{F}}
.

In this section we define a functor

V^{G}:G\mathbf{Orb}^{{\mathtt{comp}}}\to\mathrm{KK}^{G}

which will be used as an ingredient of the definition of the functor
\mathrm{K}^{G}_{A}
in
Section

3.2
below.

By
G\mathrm{LCH}_{+}
we denote the category of locally compact Hausdorff spaces with continuous
G
-action and
partially defined proper maps. We have a functor

C_{0}:G\mathrm{LCH}_{+}^{\mathrm{op}}\to GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}

which sends a locally compact Hausdorff
G
-space
X
to the
G
-
C^{*}
-algebra
C_{0}(X)
of complex functions on
X
vanishing at
\infty
.
By Gelfand duality it identifies
G\mathrm{LCH}_{+}^{\mathrm{op}}
with the full subcategory of
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
of commutative
G
-
C^{*}
-algebras.

The category
G\mathrm{LCH}_{+}
has a topological enrichment such that for any compact Hausdorff space

W
we have

{\mathtt{Hom}}_{{\mathbf{Top}}}(W,{\mathtt{Hom}}_{G\mathrm{LCH}_{+}}(X,Y))\cong{\mathtt{Hom}}_{G\mathrm{LCH}_{+}}(W\times X,Y)\ .

The enrichment provides a notion of homotopy equivalence. We
let

L_{h}:G\mathrm{LCH}_{+}\to G\mathrm{LCH}_{+,h}

denote the Dwyer-Kan localization of
G\mathrm{LCH}_{+}
at the homotopy equivalences.
By the homotopy invariance of the functor
\mathrm{kk}^{G}
the composition

G\mathrm{LCH}_{+}^{\mathrm{op}}\xrightarrow{C_{0}}GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{kk}^{G}}\mathrm{KK}^{G}

sends homotopy equivalences to equivalences. The universal property of the Dwyer-Kan localization
L_{h}
then provides a factorization of this composition through a functor
indicated by the dotted arrow in

\textstyle{G\mathrm{LCH}_{+}^{\mathrm{op}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{C_{0}}
\scriptstyle{L_{h}}
\textstyle{GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{kk}^{G}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{KK}^{G}}
\textstyle{G\mathrm{LCH}_{+,h}^{\mathrm{op}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{kk}^{G}C_{0}(-)}

(3.1)

We let

\underline{\mathrm{KK}^{G}}:\mathrm{KK}^{G,\mathrm{op}}\times\mathrm{KK}^{G}\to\mathrm{KK}^{G}

denote the internal
{\mathtt{Hom}}
-functor of the closed symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category
\mathrm{KK}^{G}
.
We can restrict the functor
\mathrm{kk}^{G}C_{0}
along the fully faithful inclusion
G\mathbf{Orb}^{{\mathtt{comp}}}\to G\mathrm{LCH}_{+,h}
.

Definition 3.1
.

We define the functor

V^{G}(-):=\underline{\mathrm{KK}^{G}}(\mathrm{kk}^{G}C_{0}(-),{\mathbf{1}}_{\mathrm{KK}^{G}}):G\mathbf{Orb}^{{\mathtt{comp}}}\to\mathrm{KK}^{G}

(3.2)

Remark 3.2
.

If
G
is not compact, then not every map between
G
-orbits is proper.
But the restriction to compact orbits in
3.1
ensures this properness.
The other option would be to restrict along the inclusion
G_{\mathcal{C}\mathrm{omp}}\mathbf{Orb}\to G\mathrm{LCH}_{+,h}
, where
\mathcal{C}\mathrm{omp}
denotes the family of compact subgroups.
In the case of discrete groups the resulting functor

\underline{\mathrm{KK}^{G}}(\mathrm{kk}^{G}C_{0}(-),{\mathbf{1}}_{\mathrm{KK}^{G}}):G_{\mathcal{C}\mathrm{omp}}\mathbf{Orb}\to\mathrm{KK}^{G}

turns out to be equivalent, as a consequence of Paschke duality, to the functor
denoted by
\mathrm{kk}^{G}_{C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}}\circ{\mathbb{C}}[-]
in
[
BELa
, Sec. 16]
.
The latter is actually defined on the whole orbit category
G\mathbf{Orb}
.
For discrete
G
and
A
in
\mathrm{KK}^{G}
the functor

K^{G}_{A}:=\mathrm{K}^{G}(A\otimes\mathrm{kk}^{G}_{C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}}\circ{\mathbb{C}}[-]):G\mathbf{Orb}\to{\mathbf{Mod}}(KU)

is the functor
featuring the Baum-Connes conjecture in
1.2
.

If
G
is not compact, then
G_{\mathcal{C}\mathrm{omp}}\mathbf{Orb}\cap G\mathbf{Orb}^{{\mathtt{comp}}}=\emptyset
.
Hence we can not use the results from
[
BELa
, Sec. 16]
in order to extend the functor
V^{G}
.

∎

If
S
in
G\mathbf{Orb}^{{\mathtt{comp}}}
is such that
\mathrm{kk}^{G}C_{0}(S)
is dualizable in
\mathrm{KK}^{G}
, then by definition

V^{G}(S)
is the dual of
\mathrm{kk}^{G}C_{0}(S)
. The following two propositions refine this observation.

Note the restriction to finite orbits in the following statement.

Proposition 3.3
.

For every
S
in
G\mathbf{Orb}^{\mathrm{fin}}
the object
\mathrm{kk}^{G}C_{0}(S)
of
\mathrm{KK}^{G}
is selfdual.

Proof.

We consider

S=G/H
in
G\mathbf{Orb}^{\mathrm{fin}}
. Note that
C_{0}(S)\cong{\mathtt{Ind}}_{H}^{G}(\mathrm{Res}_{H}({\mathbb{C}}))
,
{\mathbf{1}}_{\mathrm{KK}^{H}}\simeq\mathrm{kk}^{H}(\mathrm{Res}_{H}({\mathbb{C}}))
, and therefore

\mathrm{kk}^{G}C_{0}(S)\simeq{\mathtt{Ind}}_{H}^{G}({\mathbf{1}}_{\mathrm{KK}^{H}})
. Using this observation the selfduality of

\mathrm{kk}^{G}C_{0}(S)
is a formal consequence of the some of the formulas listed in
Section

2.1
.
We have the following chain of equivalences which is natural in
A,B
in
\mathrm{KK}^{G}
:

\displaystyle\mathrm{KK}^{G}(B,\mathrm{kk}^{G}C_{0}(S)\otimes A)

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\mathrm{KK}^{G}(B,{\mathtt{Ind}}_{H}^{G}({\mathbf{1}}_{\mathrm{KK}^{H}})\otimes A)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{dfbvdfvdfvdfvsdfv2}}}{{\simeq}}

\displaystyle\mathrm{KK}^{G}(B,{\mathtt{Ind}}_{H}^{G}(\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{H}(A)))

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{dfbvdfvdfvdfvsdfv1}}}{{\simeq}}

\displaystyle\mathrm{KK}^{H}(\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{H}(B),\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{H}(A))

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{dfbvdfvdfvdfvsdfv}}}{{\simeq}}

\displaystyle\mathrm{KK}^{G}({\mathtt{Ind}}_{H}^{G}(\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{H}(B)),A)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{dfbvdfvdfvdfvsdfv2}}}{{\simeq}}

\displaystyle\mathrm{KK}^{G}({\mathtt{Ind}}_{H}^{G}({\mathbf{1}}_{\mathrm{KK}^{H}})\otimes B,A)

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\mathrm{KK}^{G}(\mathrm{kk}^{G}C_{0}(S)\otimes B,A)

Note that we must assume that
S
is finite since this is required for the adjunction (
2.10
) used in the chain above. This condition also implies that
H
is open which is necessary for the application of
(
2.9
).∎

Remark 3.4
.

By
2.6
we have a tensor normed functor

(\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{H},\mathrm{Nm}^{G}_{H})
. We could conclude the self-duality of
{\mathtt{Ind}}_{H}^{G}(\mathrm{Res}_{H}^{G}({\mathbf{1}}_{\mathrm{KK}}))\simeq\mathrm{kk}^{G}C_{0}(G/H)
from
[
CSY22
, 2.3.4, (3)]
(which has essentially the same proof as given above). We write out the details since they will be used in the proof
3.5

asserting an equivariant refinement of this self-duality.

∎

By functoriality we can consider
V^{G}(S)
and
\mathrm{kk}^{G}C_{0}(S)
both as objects in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(B{\mathtt{Aut}}_{G\mathbf{Orb}}(S),\mathrm{KK}^{G})
.

Proposition 3.5
.

For every
S
in
G\mathbf{Orb}^{\mathrm{fin}}
we have a canonical equivalence

V^{G}(S)\simeq\mathrm{kk}^{G}C_{0}(S)

(3.3)

in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(B{\mathtt{Aut}}_{G\mathbf{Orb}}(S),\mathrm{KK}^{G})
.

Proof.

Note that
V^{G}(S)
is by (
3.2
) the dual to
\mathrm{kk}^{G}C_{0}(S)
. If one ignores the
B{\mathtt{Aut}}_{G\mathbf{Orb}}(S)
-actions, then
such an equivalence (
3.3
) is given by
3.3
.
Our task is to refine the selfduality of
\mathrm{kk}^{G}C_{0}(S)
to a
B{\mathtt{Aut}}_{G\mathbf{Orb}}(S)
-equivariant selfduality.
The given proof of
3.3
does not make this equivariance apparent.

The selfduality of
\mathrm{kk}^{G}C_{0}(S)
is equivalent to an adjunction

-\otimes\mathrm{kk}^{G}C_{0}(S):\mathrm{KK}^{G}\leftrightarrows\mathrm{KK}^{G}:-\otimes\mathrm{kk}^{G}C_{0}(S)\ .

(3.4)

Such an adjunction is fixed by the unit which is a natural transformation

\epsilon:{\mathtt{id}}_{\mathrm{KK}^{G}}\to(-\otimes\mathrm{kk}^{G}C(S))\otimes\mathrm{kk}^{G}C(S)

of endofunctors of
\mathrm{KK}^{G}
. In the present case this unit is given
by the tensor product with a morphism

{\mathbf{1}}_{\mathrm{KK}^{G}}\to\mathrm{kk}^{G}C_{0}(S)\otimes\mathrm{kk}^{G}C_{0}(S)

in
\mathrm{KK}^{G}
.
Equivariance of the adjunction is then equivalent to a refinement of this morphism to a morphism in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(B{\mathtt{Aut}}_{G\mathbf{Orb}}(S),\mathrm{KK}^{G})
.

Below we will show that the morphism given by the proof of
3.3

is induced by
the map of
C^{*}
-algebras

{\mathbb{C}}\to C_{0}(S\times S)\cong C_{0}(S)\otimes C_{0}(S)\ ,\quad\lambda\mapsto\lambda\chi_{{\mathtt{diag}}(S)}\ ,

(3.5)

where
\chi_{{\mathtt{diag}}(S)}
is the characteristic function of the diagonal in
S
.
This map is obviously
B{\mathtt{Aut}}_{G\mathbf{Orb}}(S)
-equivariant.

We assume that
S=G/H
.
We already know that
\mathrm{kk}^{G}C_{0}(G/H)
is self dual by
3.3
. Then unit of this selfduality is the image of
{\mathtt{id}}_{\mathrm{kk}^{G}C_{0}(G/H)}
under the duality

\mathrm{KK}^{G}(\mathrm{kk}^{G}C_{0}(G/H),\mathrm{kk}^{G}C_{0}(G/H))\simeq\mathrm{KK}^{G}({\mathbf{1}}_{\mathrm{KK}^{G}},\mathrm{kk}^{G}C_{0}(G/H)\otimes\mathrm{kk}^{G}C_{0}(G/H))

given by the chain

\displaystyle\mathrm{KK}^{G}(\mathrm{kk}^{G}C_{0}(G/H),\mathrm{kk}^{G}C_{0}(G/H))

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\mathrm{KK}^{G}({\mathtt{Ind}}_{H}^{G}({\mathbf{1}}_{\mathrm{KK}^{H}}),\mathrm{kk}^{G}C_{0}(G/H))

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\mathrm{KK}^{H}({\mathbf{1}}_{\mathrm{KK}^{H}},\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{H}(\mathrm{kk}^{G}C_{0}(G/H)))

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\mathrm{KK}^{H}(\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{H}({\mathbf{1}}_{\mathrm{KK}^{G}}),\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{H}(\mathrm{kk}^{G}C_{0}(G/H)))

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\mathrm{KK}^{G}({\mathbf{1}}_{\mathrm{KK}^{G}},{\mathtt{Ind}}_{H}^{G}(\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{H}(\mathrm{kk}^{G}C_{0}(G/H))))

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\mathrm{KK}^{G}({\mathbf{1}}_{\mathrm{KK}^{G}},\mathrm{kk}^{G}C_{0}(G/H)\otimes\mathrm{kk}^{G}C_{0}(G/H))

of equivalences in the proof of
3.3
.
We must show that this image of
{\mathtt{id}}_{\mathrm{kk}^{G}C_{0}(G/H)}
is induced by the map
which sends
\lambda
in
{\mathbb{C}}
to
\lambda\chi_{{\mathtt{diag}}(G/H)}
.
As explained in
Section

2.1
all these equivalences are induced by explicit maps on the level of morphims sets of
C^{*}
-algebras:

\displaystyle{\mathtt{Hom}}_{GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}}(C_{0}(G/H),C_{0}(G/H))

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle{\mathtt{Hom}}_{GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}}({\mathtt{Ind}}_{H}^{G}(\mathrm{Res}_{H}({\mathbb{C}})),C_{0}(G/H))

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle 1}}{{\to}}

\displaystyle{\mathtt{Hom}}_{HC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}}(\mathrm{Res}_{H}({\mathbb{C}}),\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{H}(C_{0}(G/H)))

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle{\mathtt{Hom}}_{HC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}}(\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{H}(\mathrm{Res}_{G}({\mathbb{C}})),\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{H}(C_{0}(G/H)))

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle 2}}{{\to}}

\displaystyle{\mathtt{Hom}}_{GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}}(\mathrm{Res}_{G}({\mathbb{C}}),{\mathtt{Ind}}_{H}^{G}(\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{H}(C_{0}(G/H))))

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle{\mathtt{Hom}}_{GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}}(\mathrm{Res}_{G}({\mathbb{C}}),C_{0}(G/H\times G/H))

The map marked by
1
is given by applying
\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{H}
and precomposing
with the unit
\mathrm{Res}_{H}({\mathbb{C}})\to\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{H}({\mathtt{Ind}}^{G}_{H}(\mathrm{Res}_{H}({\mathbb{C}})))
of the
({\mathtt{Ind}}_{H}^{G},\mathrm{Res}_{H}^{G})
-adjunction which happens to exist on the level of algebras. This
unit sends
\lambda
to the function
\lambda\chi_{\{H\}}
.
So the image of
{\mathtt{id}}_{C_{0}(G/H)}
in the target of
1
is the map

\lambda\mapsto\lambda\chi_{\{H\}}
. The map marked by
2
is
given by applying
{\mathtt{Ind}}_{H}^{G}
and then precomposing with the unit
\mathrm{Res}_{G}({\mathbb{C}})\to{\mathtt{Ind}}_{H}^{G}(\mathrm{Res}_{H}^{G}(\mathrm{Res}_{G}({\mathbb{C}})))
of the

(\mathrm{Res}_{H}^{G},{\mathtt{Ind}}_{H}^{G})
-adjunction which again happens to exist on the level of
algebras. This map sends
\lambda
to the constant function
\lambda\chi_{G/H}
.
The image of
{\mathtt{id}}_{C_{0}(G/H)}
in the target of this map is therefore
the map
\lambda\mapsto(gH\mapsto(g^{\prime}H\mapsto\lambda\chi_{\{H\}}(g^{\prime}H)))
.
The isomorphism
{\mathtt{Ind}}_{H}^{G}(\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{H}(C_{0}(G/H)))\cong C_{0}(G/H)\times C_{0}(G/H)

sends the function
gH\mapsto(g^{\prime}H\mapsto f(gH)(g^{\prime}H))
to the function

\hat{f}:(gH,g^{\prime}H)\mapsto f(gH)(g^{-1}g^{\prime}H)
. In particular, the function
(gH,g^{\prime}H)\mapsto\chi_{\{H\}}(g^{-1}g^{\prime}H)

is the characteristic function
\chi_{{\mathtt{diag}}(G/H)}
.
∎

If
G
is compact, then since the left-adjoint of the adjunction (
2.16
) has a symmetric monoidal refinement, we obtain a lax symmetric monoidal refinement of the right-adjoint crossed product functor
-\rtimes_{r}G
.
The image of the group
C^{*}
-algebra
C_{r}^{*}(G)\cong{\mathbb{C}}\rtimes_{r}G
in
\mathrm{KK}
therefore refines to a commutative algebra object

\mathrm{R}(G):={\mathbf{1}}_{\mathrm{KK}^{G}}\rtimes_{r}G

(3.6)

in
\mathrm{KK}
, and
the reduced crossed product functor itself refines to a functor

-\rtimes_{r}G:\mathrm{KK}^{G}\to{\mathbf{Mod}}(\mathrm{R}(G))\ .

(3.7)

If
G
is finite, then
G
with the action by left translations belongs to in
G\mathbf{Orb}^{\mathrm{fin}}
and we have an isomorphism
{\mathtt{Aut}}_{G\mathbf{Orb}}(G)\cong G^{\mathrm{op}}
acting on
G
by multiplication from the right.
By functoriality,

V^{G}(G)
becomes an object of
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG^{\mathrm{op}},\mathrm{KK}^{G})
.
We define the functor

\widetilde{\mathrm{Res}}^{G}:\mathrm{KK}^{G}\to{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG^{\mathrm{op}},{\mathbf{Mod}}(\mathrm{R}(G)))\ ,\quad\widetilde{\mathrm{Res}}^{G}(-):=(-\otimes V^{G}(G))\rtimes_{r}G\ .

(3.8)

The following result shows that
\widetilde{\mathrm{Res}}^{G}
refines the restriction functor
\widehat{\mathrm{Res}}^{G}:\mathrm{KK}^{G}\to{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,\mathrm{KK})
from (
2.7
).
Let

e:{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG^{\mathrm{op}},{\mathbf{Mod}}(\mathrm{R}(G)))\to{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,\mathrm{KK})

(3.9)

be the functor which forgets the
\mathrm{R}(G)
-module structure
and precomposes with the inversion map
BG\to BG^{\mathrm{op}}
.

Lemma 3.6
.

In the case of a finite group
G

we have an equivalence

e\circ\widetilde{\mathrm{Res}}^{G}\simeq\widehat{\mathrm{Res}}^{G}

of functors from
\mathrm{KK}^{G}
to
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,\mathrm{KK})
.

Proof.

For a
G
-
C^{*}
-algebra
A
we have a natural isomorphism in
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}

A\otimes C_{0}(G)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cong}}{{\to}}\mathrm{Res}^{G}(A)\otimes C_{0}(G)\ .

Identifying elements of the tensor product with functions
f:G\to A
this isomorphism is given by

f\mapsto(g\mapsto g^{-1}f(g))
. Under this isomorphism the
G^{\mathrm{op}}
-action on the left goes to the action
(h,f)\mapsto(g\mapsto h^{-1}f(gh^{-1}))
on the right.
On the level of
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG^{\mathrm{op}},\mathrm{KK}^{G})
this isomorphism induces an equivalence of functors

-\otimes C_{0}(G)\simeq e^{{}^{\prime},*}\widehat{\mathrm{Res}}^{G}(-)\otimes C_{0}(G)\ ,

(3.10)

where
e^{\prime}:BG^{\mathrm{op}}\to BG
is induced by the inversion map.

We have the following chain of equivalences in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,\mathrm{KK})
:

\displaystyle e\circ\widetilde{\mathrm{Res}}^{G}(-)

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle e(-\otimes V^{G}(G))\rtimes_{r}G

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{dsfvdsfvdfvsdfv}}}{{\simeq}}

\displaystyle e(-\otimes\mathrm{kk}^{G}C_{0}(G))\rtimes_{r}G

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{bfsbspokvopsrwgfergvfs}}}{{\simeq}}

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathrm{Res}}^{G}(-)\otimes e(\mathrm{kk}^{G}C_{0}(G)\rtimes_{r}G)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{wtkorhopweferwfwregw}}}{{\simeq}}

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathrm{Res}}^{G}(-)

∎

3.2
The K-theory functor

We let

\mathrm{K}:\mathrm{KK}\to{\mathbf{Mod}}(KU)\ ,\quad B\mapsto{\mathtt{map}}_{\mathrm{KK}}({\mathbf{1}}_{\mathrm{KK}},B)

(3.11)

denote the lax symmetric monoidal functor corepresented by the tensor unit
{\mathbf{1}}_{\mathrm{KK}}
of
\mathrm{KK}
.
The usual
KU
-module valued
K
-theory functor for
C^{*}
-algebras is then given by the composition

K:C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{kk}}\mathrm{KK}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{K}}{\mathbf{Mod}}(KU)

(3.12)

is (see
[
BLP
]
or
[
Bun23
]
for details).
Similarly, for a second countable locally compact group
G

we define
the lax symmetric monoidal equivariant
K
-theory functor

\mathrm{K}^{G}:\mathrm{KK}^{G}\to{\mathbf{Mod}}(R(G))\ ,\quad B\mapsto{\mathtt{map}}_{\mathrm{KK}^{G}}({\mathbf{1}}_{\mathrm{KK}^{G}},B)\ .

(3.13)

For a compact group
G
, using
{\mathbf{1}}_{\mathrm{KK}^{G}}\simeq\mathrm{Res}_{G}({\mathbf{1}}_{\mathrm{KK}})
, we get the equivalence

\mathrm{K}(\mathrm{R}(G))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{erwfeqewdweqwd}}}{{\simeq}}{\mathtt{map}}_{\mathrm{KK}}({\mathbf{1}}_{\mathrm{KK}},{\mathbf{1}}_{\mathrm{KK}^{G}}\rtimes_{r}G)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{bsdfvkspdfvqreve}}}{{\simeq}}{\mathtt{map}}_{\mathrm{KK}^{G}}({\mathbf{1}}_{\mathrm{KK}^{G}},{\mathbf{1}}_{\mathrm{KK}^{G}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{lekjkqrlfqwedqewdqde}}}{{\simeq}}R(G)

in
{\mathbf{CAlg}}({\mathbf{Mod}}(KU))
.
This leads to a refinement of the functor (
3.11
) to a functor

\mathrm{K}:{\mathbf{Mod}}(\mathrm{R}(G))\to{\mathbf{Mod}}(R(G))

between module categories.
In view of the Green-Julg theorem (
2.16
) and (
3.7
) we furthermore get the equivalence of functors

\mathrm{K}^{G}(-)\simeq\mathrm{K}\circ(-\rtimes_{r}G):\mathrm{KK}^{G}\to{\mathbf{Mod}}(R(G))\ .

(3.14)

In this note the
R(G)
-module structures on the values of
K
-theory are an important detail
since we want to consider localizations and completions at elements of
\pi_{0}R(G)
.

Let
G
be a second countable locally compact group.

Definition 3.7
.

We define the functor

\mathrm{K}_{-}^{G}:\mathrm{KK}^{G}\to{\mathbf{Fun}}(G\mathbf{Orb}^{{\mathtt{comp}}},{\mathbf{Mod}}(R(G)))\ ,\quad A\mapsto(S\mapsto\mathrm{K}^{G}(A\otimes V^{G}(S)))\ .

(3.15)

Since
V^{G}(*)\simeq{\mathbf{1}}_{\mathrm{KK}^{G}}
we get the following consequence of (
3.14
).

Corollary 3.8
.

If
G
is compact, then we have a canonical equivalence

\mathrm{K}^{G}_{-}(*)\simeq\mathrm{K}(-\rtimes_{r}G)

of functors from
\mathrm{KK}^{G}
to
{\mathbf{Mod}}(R(G))
.

The following corollary says that the value of the functor
\mathrm{K}^{G}_{A}
on the
G
-orbit
G
with the induced
{\mathtt{Aut}}_{G\mathbf{Orb}^{\mathrm{fin}}}(G)
-action is equivalent, under the natural identifications, with
\mathrm{K}(A)
with the
G
-action via functoriality. Let

e:{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG^{\mathrm{op}},{\mathbf{Mod}}(R(G)))\to{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,{\mathbf{Mod}}(KU))

(3.16)

be the functor which forgets the
R(G)
-module structure
and precomposes with the inversion map
BG\to BG^{\mathrm{op}}
.

Corollary 3.9
.

In the case of a finite group
G

we have an equivalence of functors

e(\mathrm{K}^{G}_{-}(G))\simeq\mathrm{K}(\widehat{\mathrm{Res}}^{G}(-))

from
\mathrm{KK}^{G}
to

{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,{\mathbf{Mod}}(KU))
.

Proof.

We apply
\mathrm{K}
to the equivalence asserted in
3.6
. Unfolding definitions this gives precisely the assertion.
∎

Definition 3.10
.

We let
\mathrm{UCT}
denote the localizing subcategory of
\mathrm{KK}
generated by the tensor unit
{\mathbf{1}}_{\mathrm{KK}}
.

Since

\mathrm{K}({\mathbf{1}}_{\mathrm{KK}})\simeq KU
and
\mathrm{K}
preserves colimits, the restriction of the monoidal structure of
\mathrm{K}
induces an equivalence

\mathrm{K}(-)\otimes_{KU}\mathrm{K}(-)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}\mathrm{K}(-\otimes-):\mathrm{UCT}\times\mathrm{KK}\to{\mathbf{Mod}}(KU)\ .

(3.17)

Lemma 3.11
.

In the case of a compact group
G
, for
B
in
\mathrm{KK}
and
A
in
\mathrm{KK}^{G}
, and if one of
A
or
B
are in the
\mathrm{UCT}
-class, then
we have an equivalence

\mathrm{K}^{G}_{\mathrm{Res}_{G}(B)\otimes A}\simeq\mathrm{K}(B)\otimes_{KU}\mathrm{K}^{G}_{A}

of functors from
G\mathbf{Orb}
to
{\mathbf{Mod}}(R(G))
.

Proof.

The desired equivalence is given by the following chain:

\displaystyle\mathrm{K}^{G}_{\mathrm{Res}_{G}(B)\otimes A}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{getgoijif0ewrfrwef}}}{{\simeq}}

\displaystyle\mathrm{K}^{G}(\mathrm{Res}_{G}(B)\otimes A\otimes V^{G})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{kejtgprtgwegwregw}}}{{\simeq}}

\displaystyle\mathrm{K}((\mathrm{Res}_{G}(B)\otimes A\otimes V^{G})\rtimes_{r}G)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{fwerfeiuhieuworf}}}{{\simeq}}

\displaystyle\mathrm{K}(B\otimes((A\otimes V^{G})\rtimes_{r}G))

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{erthoijoeprthgetgtrgetrgetg}}}{{\simeq}}

\displaystyle\mathrm{K}(B)\otimes_{KU}\mathrm{K}((A\otimes V^{G})\rtimes_{r}G)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{kejtgprtgwegwregw}}}{{\simeq}}

\displaystyle\mathrm{K}(B)\otimes_{KU}\mathrm{K}^{G}(A\otimes V^{G})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{getgoijif0ewrfrwef}}}{{\simeq}}

\displaystyle\mathrm{K}(B)\otimes_{KU}\mathrm{K}^{G}_{A}.

The equivalence marked by (
3.17
) is clear if
B
is in
\mathrm{UCT}
. Otherwise,
since
G
is compact we know that
V^{G}(G/L)\rtimes_{r}G\simeq{\mathtt{Ind}}_{L}^{G}({\mathbf{1}}_{\mathrm{KK}^{L}})\rtimes_{r}G\simeq{\mathbf{1}}_{\mathrm{KK}^{L}}\rtimes_{r}L
is a sum of
|\hat{L}|
copies of
{\mathbf{1}}_{\mathrm{KK}}
and therefore belongs to
\mathrm{UCT}
for all closed subgroups
L
of
G
.
Since
(-\otimes V^{G}(S))\rtimes_{r}G
preserves colimits we can conclude that
(A\otimes V^{G}(S))\rtimes_{r}G
belong to
\mathrm{UCT}
for all
S
in
G\mathbf{Orb}
provided
A
does so. ∎

3.3
Group change

In this subsection we study the compatibility of
V^{G}
and
\mathrm{K}^{G}
with induction.
This will later be used in induction arguments on families of subgroups of
G
.
Let
G
be a second countable locally compact group.
Let
L
be a closed subgroup of
G
such that
G/L
is compact, and let
i:L\mathbf{Orb}^{{\mathtt{comp}}}\to G\mathbf{Orb}^{{\mathtt{comp}}}
be the induction functor given on objects by
S\mapsto G\times_{L}S
.

Lemma 3.12
.

If
G/L
is finite
then we have a canonical equivalence
i^{*}V^{G}\simeq{\mathtt{Ind}}_{L}^{G}(V^{L})
.

Proof.

We start with providing
a natural isomorphism of functors

C_{0}(G\times_{L}-)\cong{\mathtt{Ind}}_{L}^{G}(C_{0}(-))

(3.18)

on
L\mathbf{Orb}^{{\mathtt{comp}}}
with values in
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
.
Here we only need compactness of
G/L
.

Let
S
be in
L\mathbf{Orb}^{{\mathtt{comp}}}
.
Since
G/L
is compact the support condition mentioned in the text after (
2.6
) becomes irrelevant
and we have an isomorphism
{\mathtt{Ind}}_{L}^{G}(C_{0}(S))\cong C(G,C_{0}(S))^{L}
. Since
S
is compact we can identify
C(G,C_{0}(S))\cong C(G\times S)
. Under this isomorphism
the
L
-invariance condition requires that
f(gl,l^{-1}s)=f(g,s)
for all
l
in
L
. But the functions satisfying this condition
are precisely the functions which descend to the quotient
G\times S\to G\times_{L}S
. Since

G\times_{L}S
is compact we have the equality

C_{0}(G\times_{L}S)=C(G\times_{L}S)
.
This gives the desired isomorphism (
3.18
)

We now get the asserted equivalence from

\displaystyle i^{*}V^{G}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{vfsw3fsfsfg}}}{{\simeq}}

\displaystyle\underline{\mathrm{KK}}^{G}(\mathrm{kk}^{G}C_{0}(G\times_{L}-),{\mathbf{1}}_{\mathrm{KK}^{G}})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{fqoijfiofjp14orleqfwqf}}}{{\simeq}}

\displaystyle\underline{\mathrm{KK}}^{G}({\mathtt{Ind}}_{L}^{G}(\mathrm{kk}^{L}C_{0}(-)),{\mathbf{1}}_{\mathrm{KK}^{G}})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle!}}{{\simeq}}

\displaystyle{\mathtt{Ind}}_{L}^{G}(\underline{\mathrm{KK}}^{L}(\mathrm{kk}^{L}C_{0}(-)),{\mathbf{1}}_{\mathrm{KK}^{L}})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{vfsw3fsfsfg}}}{{\simeq}}

\displaystyle{\mathtt{Ind}}_{L}^{G}(V^{L})\ m,

where the marked equivalence uses the equivalence

\underline{\mathrm{KK}^{G}}({\mathtt{Ind}}_{L}^{G}(-_{1}),-_{2})\simeq{\mathtt{Ind}}_{L}^{G}(\underline{\mathrm{KK}^{L}}(-_{1},\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{L}(-_{2}))

which is a formal consequence of (
2.9
) (here we need that
L
is closed and open in
G
, or equivalently, that
G/L
is discrete), (
2.11
), the equivalence
{\mathbf{1}}_{\mathrm{KK}^{L}}\simeq\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{L}({\mathbf{1}}_{\mathrm{KK}^{G}})
, and
the defining properties of the internal
{\mathtt{Hom}}
-functors.

∎

We retain the assumption on
L
and calculate the restriction of
\mathrm{K}^{G}_{-}
along
i

in terms of
\mathrm{K}^{L}_{-}
.

Note that
\mathrm{K}^{G}_{A}
for
A
in
\mathrm{KK}^{G}
takes values in
R(G)
-modules while
\mathrm{K}^{L}_{B}
for
B
in
\mathrm{KK}^{L}
takes values in
R(L)
-modules. But since

{\mathbf{1}}_{\mathrm{KK}^{L}}\simeq\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{L}({\mathbf{1}}_{\mathrm{KK}^{G}})
the action of
\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{L}
on the endmorphism algebras of the tensor units yields
a morphism
\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{L}:R(G)\to R(L)
in
{\mathbf{CAlg}}({\mathbf{Mod}}(KU))
. We will use the corresponding restriction morphism
r^{*}:{\mathbf{Mod}}(R(L))\to{\mathbf{Mod}}(R(G))
in order to turn the values of
\mathrm{K}^{L}_{B}
into
R(G)
-modules.

Remark 3.13
.

If
G
is compact, then the induced map

\pi_{0}\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{L}:\pi_{0}R(G)\to\pi_{0}R(L)
sends the class
[V]
of a finite dimensional representation
V
of
G
to the class
[V_{|L}]
of its restriction to
L
, see
2.5
.
∎

Lemma 3.14
.

If
G/L
is finite, then an equivalence of functors

i^{*}\mathrm{K}^{G}_{-}\simeq r^{*}\mathrm{K}^{L}_{\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{L}(-)}:\mathrm{KK}^{G}\times G\mathbf{Orb}^{{\mathtt{comp}}}\to{\mathbf{Mod}}(R(G))\ .

Proof.

The desired equivalence is given by the following chain:

\displaystyle i^{*}\mathrm{K}^{G}_{-}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{getgoijif0ewrfrwef}}}{{\simeq}}

\displaystyle\mathrm{K}^{G}(-\otimes i^{*}V^{G})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{weijogpwergwergf9}}}{{\simeq}}

\displaystyle\mathrm{K}^{G}(-\otimes{\mathtt{Ind}}_{L}^{G}(V^{L}))

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{dfbvdfvdfvdfvsdfv2}}}{{\simeq}}

\displaystyle\mathrm{K}^{G}({\mathtt{Ind}}_{L}^{G}(V^{L}\otimes\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{L}(-))

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{werfwerfreffwefrefwrefwref}}}{{\simeq}}

\displaystyle{\mathtt{map}}_{\mathrm{KK}^{G}}({\mathbf{1}}_{\mathrm{KK}^{G}},{\mathtt{Ind}}_{L}^{G}(V^{L}\otimes\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{L}(-)))

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{dfbvdfvdfvdfvsdfv1}}}{{\simeq}}

\displaystyle r^{*}{\mathtt{map}}_{\mathrm{KK}^{L}}({\mathbf{1}}_{\mathrm{KK}^{L}},V^{L}\otimes\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{L}(-))

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{werfwerfreffwefrefwrefwref}}}{{\simeq}}

\displaystyle r^{*}\mathrm{K}^{L}_{\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{L}(-)}

Note that we must assume that
G/L
is finite since this is required for the adjunction
(
2.10
) and
3.12
used in the chain above.
∎

If we combine
3.14
and
3.8
we can calculate some values of the functor
\mathrm{K}^{G}_{A}
.

Corollary 3.15
.

If
G
is compact and

G/L
is finite, then for every
A
in
\mathrm{KK}^{G}
we have an equivalence

\mathrm{K}^{G}_{A}(G/L)\simeq r^{*}\mathrm{K}(\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{L}(A)\rtimes_{r}L)

of
R(G)
-modules.

We consider a closed subgroup
L
of
G
and
B
in
\mathrm{KK}^{L}
.

Proposition 3.16
.

If
G
is finite, then we have an equivalence

\mathrm{K}^{G}_{{\mathtt{Ind}}_{L}^{G}(B)}\simeq i_{!}\mathrm{K}^{L}_{B}
of functors from
G\mathbf{Orb}
to
{\mathbf{Mod}}(KU)
.

Proof.

We have the following chain of equivalences:

\displaystyle\mathrm{K}^{G}_{{\mathtt{Ind}}_{L}^{G}(B)}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{getgoijif0ewrfrwef}}}{{\simeq}}

\displaystyle\mathrm{K}^{G}({\mathtt{Ind}}_{L}^{G}(B)\otimes V^{G})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{dfbvdfvdfvdfvsdfv2}}}{{\simeq}}

\displaystyle\mathrm{K}^{G}({\mathtt{Ind}}_{L}^{G}(B\otimes\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{L}(V^{G})))

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{dfbvdfvdfvdfvsdfv1}}}{{\simeq}}

\displaystyle\mathrm{K}^{L}(B\otimes\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{L}(V^{G}))

We can apply (
2.10
) since
G
is finite.

Let
j^{G}:G\mathbf{Orb}\to G{\mathbf{Set}}^{\mathrm{fin}}
be the inclusion of the orbit category into the category of finite
G
-sets and equivariant maps. We consider the functor

\hat{V}^{G}:G{\mathbf{Set}}^{\mathrm{fin}}\to\mathrm{KK}^{G}\ ,\quad S\mapsto\underline{\mathrm{KK}}^{G}(\mathrm{kk}^{G}C_{0}(-),{\mathbf{1}}_{\mathrm{KK}^{G}})\ .

We then have a canonical equivalence
j^{G,*}\hat{V}^{G}\simeq V^{G}
, i.e., the functor
\hat{V}^{G}
is an extension of
V^{G}
from transitive
G
-sets to all finite
G
-sets.

We have an adjunction

\hat{i}:L{\mathbf{Set}}^{\mathrm{fin}}\leftrightarrows G{\mathbf{Set}}^{\mathrm{fin}}:r

where
r
restricts the
G
-action on an
L
-action, and

\hat{i}(S):=G\times_{L}S
is the extension of
i
to finite
G
-sets. This implies an equivalence
\hat{i}_{!}\simeq r^{*}
.

The isomorphism
\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{L}(C_{0}(-))\cong j^{G,*}r^{*}C_{0}(-)
of functors from
G\mathbf{Orb}^{\mathrm{fin}}
to
LC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
induces an equivalence
\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{L}(V^{G})\simeq j^{G,*}r^{*}\hat{V}^{L}
and consequently

\displaystyle\mathrm{K}^{L}(B\otimes\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{L}(V^{G}))

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\mathrm{K}^{L}(B\otimes j^{G,*}r^{*}\hat{V}^{L})

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle j^{G,*}r^{*}\mathrm{K}^{L}(B\otimes\hat{V}^{L})

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle j^{G,*}\hat{i}_{!}\mathrm{K}^{L}(B\otimes\hat{V}^{L})

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle i_{!}j^{L,*}\mathrm{K}^{L}(B\otimes\hat{V}^{L})

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle i_{!}K^{L}_{B}\ .

Here we have used that the canonical map
i_{!}j^{L,*}F\to j^{G,*}\hat{i}_{!}F
is an equivalence
for any coproduct preserving functor
F
on
G{\mathbf{Set}}^{\mathrm{fin}}
.
∎

Proposition 3.17

(
1.10
)
.

If
G
is finite and
A
is in
\langle{\mathtt{Ind}}_{\mathcal{P}\mathrm{rp}}^{G}\rangle
, then the assembly map is an equivalence

\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{G_{\mathcal{P}\mathrm{rp}}\mathbf{Orb}}\mathrm{K}^{G}_{A}\xrightarrow{\simeq}\mathrm{K}(A\rtimes_{r}G)\ .

(3.19)

Proof.

Since the assembly map is the evaluation at
A
of a natural transformation between colimit preserving functors on
\mathrm{KK}^{G}
it suffices to verify the equivalence (
1.8
) for

A={\mathtt{Ind}}_{L}^{G}(B)
for some proper subgroup
L
of
G
and
B
in
\mathrm{KK}^{L}
.
We use the factorization

i:L\mathbf{Orb}\xrightarrow{i^{\prime}}G_{\mathcal{P}\mathrm{rp}}\mathbf{Orb}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle i^{\mathcal{P}\mathrm{rp}}}}{{\to}}G\mathbf{Orb}\ .

(3.20)

The assembly map in the assertion of
1.10
factorizes as the following chain of equivalences:

\displaystyle\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{G_{\mathcal{P}\mathrm{rp}}\mathbf{Orb}}\mathrm{K}^{G}_{{\mathtt{Ind}}_{L}^{G}(B)}

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle(i^{\mathcal{P}\mathrm{rp}}_{!}\mathrm{K}^{G}_{{\mathtt{Ind}}_{L}^{G}(B)})(*)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{ijrogergferwg9}}}{{\simeq}}

\displaystyle(i^{\mathcal{P}\mathrm{rp}}_{!}i^{\prime}_{!}\mathrm{K}^{L}_{B})(*)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{qefewdedqewqf}}}{{\simeq}}

\displaystyle(i_{!}\mathrm{K}^{L}_{B})(*)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{ijrogergferwg9}}}{{\simeq}}

\displaystyle\mathrm{K}^{G}_{{\mathtt{Ind}}_{L}^{G}(B)}(*)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{fkjqnrhiofqwefewfqd1}}}{{\simeq}}

\displaystyle\mathrm{K}(B\rtimes_{r}G)\ ,

where the first equivalence is an instance of the pointwise formula for the left Kan-extension
i^{\mathcal{P}\mathrm{rp}}_{!}

and the identification of
G_{\mathcal{P}\mathrm{rp}}\mathbf{Orb}
with the slice of
i^{\mathcal{P}\mathrm{rp}}
over
*
.
∎

We consider a finite group
G
. Recall the functor

\mathrm{Cof}^{G}:\mathrm{KK}^{G}\to{\mathbf{Mod}}(R(G))

from (
1.7
). If
H
is a subgroup of
G
, then we obtain the faithful isofibration of finite
connected groupids
G/H\curvearrowleft G\to*\curvearrowleft G
. We then have by
2.21
a multiplicative induction functor

(-)^{\otimes G/H}_{\mathrm{kk}}:\mathrm{KK}^{H}\to\mathrm{KK}^{G}\ .

Note that this induction functor is not necessarily exact, but preserves filtered colimits.

Proposition 3.18
.

The composition

\mathrm{Cof}^{G}\circ(-)^{\otimes G/H}_{\mathrm{kk}}:\mathrm{KK}^{H}\to{\mathbf{Mod}}(R(G))

preserves all colimits.

Proof.

The composition preserves filtered colimits since
\mathrm{Cof}^{G}
and
(-)^{\otimes G/H}_{\mathrm{kk}}
do so.
In particular it sends zero algebras to zero objects. It remains to show
exactness. It suffices to show that the composition

P:\mathrm{KK}^{H}_{\mathrm{sep}}\xrightarrow{(-)^{\otimes G/H}_{\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}}}\mathrm{KK}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}\xrightarrow{y^{G}}\mathrm{KK}^{G}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{Cof}^{G}}{\mathbf{Mod}}(R(G))

is exact. To this end it suffices to show that it sends all semisplit
exact sequences
0\to A\to B\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\pi}}{{\to}}C\to 0
in
HC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}
to fibre sequences.
To this end we must show that the canonical map

P(A)\to{\mathrm{Fib}}(P(B)\to P(C))

is an equivalence. We will employ the filtration
(I_{m})_{m=0,\dots,|G/H|}
of
B^{\otimes G/H}

from step
3
of the proof of
2.19
.
Since

0\to I_{1}\to B^{\otimes G/H}\to C^{\otimes G/H}\to 0

is exact and semisplit we have a fibre sequence

\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}(I_{1})\to\mathrm{kk}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}(B^{\otimes G/H})\to\mathrm{kk}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}(C^{\otimes G/H})\ .

Applying
\mathrm{Cof}^{G}\circ y^{G}
we see that we must show that the map

P(A)\to\mathrm{Cof}^{G}(\mathrm{kk}^{G}(I_{1}))
is an equivalence.
Since
A^{\otimes G/H}\cong I_{|G/H|}
the map

P(A)\to\mathrm{Cof}^{G}(\mathrm{kk}^{G}(I_{|G/H|}))
is an equivalence.
We now argue by downward induction on
m
that

P(A)\to\mathrm{Cof}^{G}(\mathrm{kk}^{G}(I_{m}))
is an equivalence for all
m
in
\{1,\dots,|G/H|\}
. The case of
m=1
is the desired assertion. For
1\leq m<|G|
the map in question has a factorization

P(A)\to\mathrm{Cof}^{G}(\mathrm{kk}^{G}(I_{m+1}))\to\mathrm{Cof}^{G}(\mathrm{kk}^{G}(I_{m}))\ .

The first map is an equivalence by the induction hypothesis. In order to show that the second map
is an equivalence we
use the semisplit exact exact sequence (
2.34
) which
induces a fibre sequence

\mathrm{Cof}^{G}(\mathrm{kk}^{G}(I_{m+1}))\to\mathrm{Cof}^{G}(\mathrm{kk}^{G}(I_{m}))\to\mathrm{Cof}^{G}(\mathrm{kk}^{G}(I_{m}/I_{m+1}))

Using the notation from (
2.35
) we observe that

I_{m}/I_{m+1}\cong\bigoplus_{[F]\in\mathcal{P}_{m}(Z)/G}{\mathtt{Ind}}_{\mathrm{sep},G_{F}}^{G}(A^{\otimes F}\otimes C^{\otimes Z\setminus F})\ .

(3.21)

Since
1\leq m<|G/H|
all stabilizer subgroups
G_{F}
are proper subgroups of
G
. Thus

\mathrm{kk}^{G}(I_{m}/I_{m+1})
belongs to
\langle{\mathtt{Ind}}_{\mathcal{P}\mathrm{rp}}^{G}\rangle
. By
1.10
we have

\mathrm{Cof}^{G}(\mathrm{kk}^{G}(I_{m}/I_{m+1}))\simeq 0
which completes the induction step.
∎

4
KK-valued homology theories

4.1
Localization and completion

In this section we recall some generalities on completion and localizations.

Let
{\mathbf{C}}
be any presentably symmetric monoidal stable
\infty
-category. Our examples for
{\mathbf{C}}
are
\mathbf{Sp}
,
\mathrm{KK}^{G}
, or
{\mathbf{Mod}}(R(G))
. We write
{\mathbf{1}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}
for the tensor unit of
{\mathbf{C}}
. Then
R:={\mathtt{map}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}({\mathbf{1}}_{{\mathbf{C}}},{\mathbf{1}}_{{\mathbf{C}}})
is a commutative algebra in
\mathbf{Sp}
.

Let
{\mathbf{D}}
be a
{\mathbf{Mod}}(R)
-module in presentably symmetric monoidal stable
\infty
-categories.
The basic example is
{\mathbf{C}}
itself.
In the case of spectra
\mathbf{Sp}
the tensor unit
{\mathbf{1}}_{\mathbf{Sp}}
and
R
are both the sphere spectrum
S
and

\mathbf{Sp}\simeq{\mathbf{Mod}}(S)
.

If we apply this to the case
{\mathbf{C}}=\mathrm{KK}^{G}
, then
R\simeq R(G)
.
If
H\to G
is a homomorphism, then we can consider

\mathrm{KK}^{H}
as a
{\mathbf{Mod}}(R(G))
-module in place of
{\mathbf{D}}
using the restriction map
R(G)\to R(H)
.

We fix a class
\xi
in
\pi_{0}R
.
We write
\xi:{\mathtt{id}}_{{\mathbf{D}}}\to{\mathtt{id}}_{{\mathbf{D}}}
also for the endomorphism of the identity functor
{\mathtt{id}}_{{\mathbf{D}}}
whose component at the object
D
is
given by

D\simeq{\mathbf{1}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}\otimes D\xrightarrow{\xi\otimes{\mathtt{id}}_{D}}{\mathbf{1}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}\otimes D\simeq D\ ,

where
\otimes
denotes the structure map
{\mathbf{C}}\times{\mathbf{D}}\to{\mathbf{D}}
of the module structure and the two equivalences are given its the unit constraint.

Definition 4.1
.

1.

We define the
\xi
-torsion functor by

S_{\xi}:=\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{n\in{\rm Nature}}{\mathrm{Fib}}({\mathtt{id}}_{{\mathbf{D}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\xi^{n}}}{{\to}}{\mathtt{id}}_{{\mathbf{D}}}):{\mathbf{D}}\to{\mathbf{D}}\ .

2.

We define the inversion of
\xi
by

(-)[\xi^{-1}]:=\mathrm{Cofib}(S_{\xi}\to{\mathtt{id}}_{{\mathbf{D}}})\simeq\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{n\in{\rm Nature}}({\mathtt{id}}_{{\mathbf{D}}}\xrightarrow{\xi}{\mathtt{id}}_{{\mathbf{D}}}\xrightarrow{\xi}{\mathtt{id}}_{{\mathbf{D}}}\xrightarrow{\xi}\dots):{\mathbf{D}}\to{\mathbf{D}}\ .

By construction we have a fibre sequence of endofunctors

S_{\xi}\to{\mathtt{id}}_{{\mathbf{D}}}\to(-)[\xi^{-1}]

(4.1)

of
{\mathbf{D}}
.

The commutative algebra
R
considered as an
R
-module is an object of
{\mathbf{Mod}}(R)
so that we can consider
S_{\xi}(R)
in
{\mathbf{Mod}}(R)
.
Let
D
be an object of
{\mathbf{D}}
.

Definition 4.2
.

1.

D
is called
\xi
-acyclic if
S_{\xi}(R)\otimes D\simeq 0
.

2.

D
is called
\xi
-complete if
{\mathtt{map}}_{{\mathbf{D}}}(D^{\prime},D)\simeq 0
for all
\xi
-acyclic objects
D^{\prime}
of
{\mathbf{D}}
.

The following assertion is well-known. The case of
{\mathbf{C}}=\mathbf{Sp}
and
\xi
a prime in
\pi_{0}S\cong{\mathbb{Z}}
is considered in
[
Bou79
]
, and the general case is completely analogous.

Proposition 4.3
.

1.

There exists a Bousfield localization

L_{\xi}:{\mathbf{D}}\leftrightarrows L_{\xi}{\mathbf{D}}:\mathrm{incl}\ ,

where
L_{\xi}{\mathbf{D}}
consists of the
\xi
-complete objects, and where the kernel of
L_{\xi}
consists precisely of the
\xi
-acyclic objects.

2.

An object
D
in
{\mathbf{D}}
is
\xi
-acyclic if and only if
\xi
acts on
D
as an equivalence.

3.

For any object
D
in
{\mathbf{D}}
we have an equivalence

L_{\xi}(D)\simeq\operatorname*{\mathtt{lim}}_{n\in{\rm Nature}}D/\xi^{n}\ ,

where
D/\xi^{n}:=\mathrm{Cofib}(D\xrightarrow{\xi^{n}}D)
.

4.2
Two homological functors

Let
G
be a second countable locally compact topological group and
recall the functor
\mathrm{kk}^{G}C_{0}:G\mathrm{LCH}_{+}^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathrm{KK}^{G}
from (
3.1
). This functor is homotopy invariant and excisive for invariant split-closed decompositions in the sense that for any decomposition of
X
in
G\mathrm{LCH}_{+}^{\mathrm{op}}
into two invariant split-closed subspaces
Y
and
Z
(see
[
BELb
, Def. 1.11]
) we have a pull-back square

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 26.23871pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-26.23871pt\raise 50.23871pt\raise-32.91226pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathrm{kk}^{G}C_{0}(Y\cap Z)}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\ .

These assertions follow from
[
BELb
, Thm. 5.2]
.

Definition 4.4

(
[
BELb
, Def. 1.14]
)
.

We define the
\mathrm{KK}^{G}
-valued analytic
K
-homology functor by

L^{G}:=\underline{\mathrm{KK}^{G}}(\mathrm{kk}^{G}C_{0}(-),{\mathbf{1}}_{\mathrm{KK}^{G}}):G\mathrm{LCH}_{+}\to\mathrm{KK}^{G}\ .

It is homotopy invariant and excisive for invariant split-closed decompositions (with the obvious modification of the definition for covariant functors).

Let
\ell:{\mathbf{Top}}\to\mathbf{Spc}
be the usual presentation of the
\infty
-category of spaces as the Dwyer-Kan localization of
{\mathbf{Top}}
at the weak equivalences. We consider
G{\mathbf{Top}}
as a topological enriched category. If we insert a
G
-space
X
into the second argument functor

{\mathtt{Map}}_{G{\mathbf{Top}}}(-,-):G{\mathbf{Top}}^{\mathrm{op}}\times G{\mathbf{Top}}\to{\mathbf{Top}}
, then we get a continuous functor
{\mathtt{Map}}_{G{\mathbf{Top}}}(-,X):G{\mathbf{Top}}\to{\mathbf{Top}}
.
After composition with
\ell
its restriction to
G
-orbits induces
a functor

\ell^{G}:G{\mathbf{Top}}\to{\mathbf{PSh}}(G\mathbf{Orb})\ ,\quad X\mapsto(S\mapsto\ell{\mathtt{Map}}_{G{\mathbf{Top}}}(S,X))\ .

(4.2)

Recall that an equivariant weak equivalence in
G{\mathbf{Top}}
is a map
X\to Y
which induces a weak equivalence

X^{H}\to Y^{H}
of fixed point spaces for all closed subgroups
H
of
G
. By Elmendorf’s theorem
\ell^{G}
presents its target as the Dwyer-Kan localization of
G{\mathbf{Top}}
at the weak equivalences.
We can consider
\ell^{G}
as the universal (unstable) equivariant homology theory.

Note that
\mathrm{KK}^{G}
is cocomplete.
By the universal property of the Yoneda embedding

y
we
get a canonical factorization

\textstyle{G\mathbf{Orb}^{{\mathtt{comp}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{y}
\scriptstyle{V^{G}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{KK}^{G}}
\textstyle{{\mathbf{PSh}}(G\mathbf{Orb}^{{\mathtt{comp}}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\hat{V}^{G}}

(4.3)

where
\hat{V}^{G}
preserves colimits.

We now assume that
G
is compact so that
G\mathbf{Orb}^{{\mathtt{comp}}}\simeq G\mathbf{Orb}
.

Definition 4.5
.

If
G
is compact, then we define the homotopy theoretic
\mathrm{KK}^{G}
-valued analytic
K
-homology as the composition

\tilde{L}^{G}:G{\mathbf{Top}}\xrightarrow{\ell^{G}}{\mathbf{PSh}}(G\mathbf{Orb})\xrightarrow{\hat{V}^{G}}\mathrm{KK}^{G}\ .

This functor is in particular
homotopy invariant and excisive for invariant open decompositions.

Remark 4.6
.

In
4.5
we must restrict to compact groups since the functor
V^{G}
is only defined on compact orbits.
In the case of discrete groups which are not necessarily finite we have an alternative definition of an equivariant homotopy theoretic
\mathrm{KK}^{G}
-valued homology theory using the functor
\mathrm{kk}^{G}_{C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}}\circ{\mathbb{C}}[-]
mentioned in
3.2
in place of
\mathrm{kk}^{G}C_{0}(-)
. This version is considered in
[
BELa
]

Let
G\mathrm{LCH}_{+}^{\mathrm{hfin}}
denote the full subcategory of
G\mathrm{LCH}_{+}
of spaces which are homotopy retracts of finite
G
-
CW
-complexes. The following results says that the analytic version of the
\mathrm{KK}^{G}
-valued equivariant
K
-homology
L^{G}
coincides with its homotopy theoretic counterpart
\tilde{L}^{G}
at least on homotopy finite spaces.

Proposition 4.7
.

We assume that
G
is compact. We have an equivalence of functors

L^{G}_{|G\mathrm{LCH}_{+}^{\mathrm{hfin}}}\simeq\tilde{L}^{G}_{|G\mathrm{LCH}_{+}^{\mathrm{hfin}}}\ .

Proof.

Both functors
L^{G}
and
\tilde{L}^{G}
are homotopy invariant and excisive for cell attachments.
The restrictions of the two functors to
G\mathbf{Orb}
are equivalent by definition.
This equivalence therefore extends essentially uniquely to an equivalence of functors on
G\mathrm{LCH}_{+}^{\mathrm{hfin}}
, see
[
BELa
, Cor. 11.9]
.
∎

4.3
A model for the classifying space of proper subgroups in homotopy theory

We assume that
G
is a finite group. We let
E_{\mathcal{P}\mathrm{rp}}G
in
{\mathbf{PSh}}(G\mathbf{Orb})
be the classifying space of proper subgroups of
G
. It is characterized by the property that

E_{\mathcal{P}\mathrm{rp}}G(G/H)\simeq\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}*&H\subsetneqq G\\
\emptyset&H=G\end{array}\right.\ .

(4.4)

In the following we provide an explicit model for
E_{\mathcal{P}\mathrm{rp}}G
.
This is a well-known construction
[
AS69
]
.

Let
V
be the orthogonal complement of the trivial representation in the complex Hilbert space
L^{2}(G)
. Let
V^{n}:=\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}V
and consider the canonical embeddings
V^{n}\to V^{n+1}
. For any finite-dimensional unitary representation
W
of
G
we consider the unit sphere
S(W)
as a compact
G
-topological space in
G{\mathbf{Top}}
. Recall the functor
\ell^{G}
from (
4.2
).

Proposition 4.8
.

For finite
G

we have an equivalence

E_{\mathcal{P}\mathrm{rp}}G:=\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{n\in{\rm Nature}}\ell^{G}(S(V^{n}))\ .

Proof.

If
H
is a proper subgroup of
G
,
then the subspace
V^{H}
of
H
-fixed vectors is non-zero.
For example, the function
\chi_{H}-\frac{|H|}{|G|}
in
L^{2}(G)
is non-zero and belongs to
V^{H}
, where
\chi_{H}
is the characteristic function of
H
. This implies that
S(V^{n})^{H}
is a sphere of dimension bigger than
n
. Since
\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{n\in{\rm Nature}}\ell(S^{n})\simeq*
we conclude that

\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{n\in{\rm Nature}}\ell(S(V^{n})^{H})\simeq*
. On the other hand
(V^{n})^{G}\cong\emptyset
so that
\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{n\in{\rm Nature}}\ell(S(V^{n})^{G})\simeq\emptyset
.

Hence
\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{n\in{\rm Nature}}\ell^{G}(S(V^{n}))
satisfies the condition
characterizing
E_{\mathcal{P}\mathrm{rp}}G
in (
4.4
).
∎

4.4
The KK-theory model for the classifying space of proper subgroups.

Recall the functor
\hat{V}^{G}:{\mathbf{PSh}}(G\mathbf{Orb})\to\mathrm{KK}^{G}
from (
4.3
).
In this section we provide a formula (
4.6
) for
\hat{V}^{G}(E_{\mathcal{P}\mathrm{rp}}G)
in
\mathrm{KK}^{G}
in terms of language introduced in
Section

4.1
.

Note that
V
is a finite-dimensional complex representation of
G
.
We consider the element

\xi:=\Lambda_{-1}(V):=\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{j}[\Lambda^{j}(V)]

(4.5)

in
\pi_{0}R(G)
,
where for any finite-dimensional
G
-representation
W
we let

[W]
denote its class in the representation ring
\pi_{0}R(G)
, see
2.5
.

Proposition 4.9
.

We assume that
G
is finite.
We have an equivalence

\hat{V}^{G}(E_{\mathcal{P}\mathrm{rp}}G)\simeq S_{\xi}({\mathbf{1}}_{\mathrm{KK}^{G}})\ .

(4.6)

Proof.

We start with analysing the effect of the inclusion maps
S(V^{n})\to S(V^{n+1})
in
\mathrm{KK}^{G}
.

Lemma 4.10
.

We have a sequence of fibre sequences

\textstyle{\vdots\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\vdots\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\vdots\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathbf{1}}_{\mathrm{KK}^{G}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\xi^{n+1}}
\scriptstyle{\xi}
\textstyle{{\mathbf{1}}_{\mathrm{KK}^{G}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\mathrm{kk}^{G}(C(S(V^{n+1})))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathbf{1}}_{\mathrm{KK}^{G}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\xi^{n}}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\mathbf{1}}_{\mathrm{KK}^{G}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{kk}^{G}(C(S(V^{n})))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\vdots}
\textstyle{\vdots}

(4.7)

Proof.

We have a map between semi-split exact sequences

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{C(B(V^{n+1}),S(V^{n+1}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

\textstyle{0}

in
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
which after application of

and some identifications explained below provides the desired map of horizontal fibre sequences.

The restriction along the embedding of the origin of
B(V^{n})
induces an equivalence

\mathrm{kk}^{G}(C(B(V^{n})))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}\mathrm{kk}^{G}({\mathbb{C}})\simeq{\mathbf{1}}_{\mathrm{KK}^{G}}

in
\mathrm{KK}^{G}
. Furthermore, we have a Bott equivalence

{\mathbf{1}}_{\mathrm{KK}^{G}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}\mathrm{kk}^{G}(C(B(V^{n}),S(V^{n})))\ .

(4.8)

Explicitly, consider the total alternating power
\Lambda^{*}V^{n}=\Lambda^{*}_{+}V^{n}\oplus\Lambda^{*}_{-}V^{n}
with the even-odd grading. We consider the equivariant bundle

and
the equivariant odd bundle

, considered as a map
\phi:B(V^{n})\to{\mathtt{End}}(\Lambda^{*}V^{n})^{\mathrm{odd}}

\phi(v):=\epsilon_{v}-i_{v}
, where

is in

,
\epsilon_{v}
is the exterior product with

, and

is the interior product using the unitary scalar product.
Then
\phi(v)^{2}=-\|v\|^{2}
. In particular
\phi
is invertible on

and
(\Lambda^{*}V^{n},\phi)
defines a class

in

. It has been shown by Kasparov

]
that this class induces the Bott equivalence (
4.8
).
The composition

{\mathbf{1}}_{\mathrm{KK}^{G}}\to\mathrm{kk}^{G}(C(B(V^{n}),S(V^{n})))\to\mathrm{kk}^{G}(C(B(V^{n}))\simeq{\mathbf{1}}_{\mathrm{KK}^{G}}

(4.9)

is then given by the multiplication by the class
\Lambda_{-1}(V^{n})
of
\Lambda^{*}V^{n}
in
R(G)
. Using the fact that
\Lambda_{-1}
turns sums into products we conclude that
(
4.9
) multiplication by
\xi^{n}=\Lambda_{-1}(V)^{n}
.
Using the isomorphism
\Lambda^{*}V^{n+1}\cong\Lambda^{*}V\otimes\Lambda^{*}V^{n}
the restriction of the relative class

of

along

(B(V^{n+1}),S(V^{n+1}))\to(B(V^{n}),S(V^{n}))
can be identified on the level of representatives with the product of the class
\beta_{V^{n}}
of

(\Lambda^{*}V^{n},\phi)
by the class of
\Lambda^{*}V
in
R(G)
, hence by
\xi
.
This explains the identification of the
left vertical map in (
4.7
).
∎

We have a chain of equivalences (natural for
n
in the poset
{\rm Nature}
)

\underline{\mathrm{KK}^{G}}(\mathrm{kk}^{G}(C(S(V^{n}))),{\mathbf{1}}_{\mathrm{KK}^{G}})\simeq L^{G}(S(V^{n}))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{wrtkjhoprgwerfwrefwerf}}}{{\simeq}}\tilde{L}^{G}(S(V^{n}))\ .

(4.10)

By

we get

\hat{V}^{G}(E_{\mathcal{P}\mathrm{rp}}G)\simeq\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{n\in{\rm Nature}}\underline{\mathrm{KK}^{G}}(\mathrm{kk}^{G}(C(S(V^{n}))),{\mathbf{1}}_{\mathrm{KK}^{G}})\ .

Applying
\underline{\mathrm{KK}^{G}}(-,{\mathbf{1}}_{\mathrm{KK}^{G}})
to the diagram (

)
and using (

\textstyle{\vdots\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

2
.

Theorem 4.11

(
Theorem

1.12
)
.

We assume that
G
is finite.
The cofibre sequence (
1.7
) is equivalent to the cofibre sequence

S_{\xi}(\mathrm{K}^{G}_{-}(*))\to\mathrm{K}^{G}_{-}(*)\to\mathrm{K}^{G}_{-}(*)[\xi^{-1}]

(4.12)

of functors from
\mathrm{KK}^{G}
to
{\mathbf{Mod}}(R(G))
.

Proof.

Evaluating (
4.1
) on
{\mathbf{1}}_{\mathrm{KK}^{G}}
we get the fibre sequence

S_{\xi}({\mathbf{1}}_{\mathrm{KK}^{G}})\to{\mathbf{1}}_{\mathrm{KK}^{G}}\to{\mathbf{1}}_{\mathrm{KK}^{G}}[\xi^{-1}]\ .

(4.13)

We apply the functor
\mathrm{K}^{G}(-\otimes A)
to the sequence
(
4.13
), where
\mathrm{K}^{G}
is the equivariant
K
-theory
from (
3.13
).
Since
\mathrm{K}^{G}(-\otimes A)
is an exact functor between
{\mathbf{Mod}}(R(G))
-modules and commutes with colimits
it commutes with the endofunctors
(-)[\xi^{-1}]
and
S_{\xi}
and we get the fibre sequence (
1.9
).

It remains to identify the first map in this fibre sequence with the first map in (
1.7
).
As seen in the proof of
4.9
the fibre sequene
(
4.13
) is equivalent to the fibre sequence

\hat{V}^{G}(E_{\mathcal{P}\mathrm{rp}}G)\to{\mathbf{1}}_{\mathrm{KK}^{G}}\to\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{{\rm Nature}}({\mathbf{1}}_{\mathrm{KK}^{G}}\xrightarrow{\xi}{\mathbf{1}}_{\mathrm{KK}^{G}}\xrightarrow{\xi}\dots)

(4.14)

If we apply
\mathrm{K}^{G}(-\otimes A)
to the fibre sequence (

), then we get a fibre sequence which is equivalent to (

), and whose first map ist

\mathrm{K}^{G}(\hat{V}^{G}(E_{\mathcal{P}\mathrm{rp}}G)\otimes A)\to\mathrm{K}^{G}_{A}(*)\ .

We now use that

E_{\mathcal{P}\mathrm{rp}}G\simeq\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{G_{\mathcal{P}\mathrm{rp}}\mathbf{Orb}}y
, where

is the Yoneda embedding.
Since
